# Uygulama durumu — .NET 8 EXE

Ana program: Windows-Current/TrMarketplaceHubDesktop.exe. Giriş parolası yoktur.

| İşlev | Durum |
|---|---|
| Mevcut masaüstü ürün/XML/kanal/sipariş ekranları | PRESERVED; API kapsamı README-YONETIM-MERKEZI.md |
| Aktif/pasif ve pasif Etsy gönderim engeli | Mevcut kod korundu |
| Ayrıntılı ürün filtreleri | IMPLEMENTED; gerçek geçici SQLite testi, 218 test toplam |
| CommerceHub merkezi stok/mağaza politikaları | NOT_PORTED |
| Normal ürün ek operasyon alanları | PARTIAL: MPN/fatura adı/alt başlık/raf/tarih taşındı; XML koruma ve kayıt testi geçti |
| Varyant sistemi | DEFERRED_BY_USER |
| Paket/set/bundle | DEFERRED_BY_USER |
| Hızlı satır içi düzenleme | DEFERRED_BY_USER |
| Kritik fiyat | DEFERRED_BY_USER |
| XML varyant mapping | DEFERRED_BY_USER |
| Fulfillment core | DEFERRED_BY_USER |
| Hakediş/mutabakat core | DEFERRED_BY_USER |

M1 Etsy dispatch scheduler (2026-09-11): IMPLEMENTED locally. WPF automation settings and due runner are connected to the idempotent sync queue. Etsy listing dispatch requires a concrete preview, unchanged product version and explicit user confirmation before PATCH. Production credential/scope validation remains environment-dependent; no live store test was run.

PR #4 review follow-up (2026-09-11): SyncJob status is now atomically claimed and marked Succeeded/Failed around Etsy dispatch. A previously succeeded job cannot dispatch again. Added fake HTTP and temporary SQLite coverage; 259 Release tests pass.

M2 Etsy order/stock (stacked on PR #4, 2026-09-11): listing detail read and explicit sale stock decision service implemented. Existing atomic receipt/idempotency path is reused; cancellation/return never restores stock automatically. Live production order scope remains LIVE_API_BLOCKED until official credentials are available.

M3 eBay safe parity (stacked on Issue #7, 2026-09-11): official Inventory/Fulfillment API read/write methods, explicit preview/approval, stale version guard and SyncJob idempotency implemented. 263 Release tests pass; production scope/credential validation remains LIVE_API_BLOCKED.

M4 marketplace adapter core (stacked on Issue #8, 2026-09-11): shared capability/preview contract, encrypted-credential-compatible local metadata boundary and channel/shop isolated mapping for Ozon, Joom, Allegro, Wish and Navlungo. Unsupported or unverified operations produce no HTTP request and remain LIVE_API_BLOCKED. 265 Release tests pass.

Ertelenen alanların tüm alt özellik/ekran/test geliştirmeleri kapsam dışıdır, eksik sayılmaz. Çalışan kod silinmez. Canlı marketplace verisi değiştirilmedi. Görsel masaüstü UI incelemesi bu adımda henüz yapılmadı.

2026-09-11: 219 test geçti. Yeni yayın Windows-Operations/TrMarketplaceHubDesktop.exe; giriş şifresi yok. Açık eski uygulama korunur. Yeni alanların görsel kontrolü yapılmadı.


Sipariş stok düşümü PARTIAL: yerel önizleme+atomik düşüm+duplicate koruma+hareket kaydı tamamlandı; rezervasyon/depo/kanallara gönderim eksik. 234 test; docs/ORDER-STOCK.md. Güncel EXE Windows-Stock/TrMarketplaceHubDesktop.exe.

Mağaza stok politikaları PARTIAL: kalıcı güvenlik stoğu/üst sınır/sürüm kontrolü ve yerel önizleme IMPLEMENTED. Dış API dispatch/depo/rezervasyon henüz yok. 235test ve Release publish başarılı. Güncel yayın Windows-Policies.

Fiyat politikası PARTIAL: yerel formül/kayıt/önizleme ve güvenlik koruması eklendi; bağımsız test/görsel kontrol ve canlı dispatch eksik. Windows-Price yayınlandı, mevcut 235 test geçti.

Excel ekranı PARTIAL: XLSX export+preview uygulandı; atomik import/kolon eşleme eksik. Windows-Excel yayınlandı.

M5 XML tedarikçi merkezi (2026-09-11, Issue #12): mevcut normal XML akışı korunarak gzip kaynak okuma ve kalıcı `XmlRuns` geçmişi eklendi. Manuel/zamanlanmış çalıştırmalar sonuç sayaçları veya hata ile kaydediliyor. XML varyant mapping `DEFERRED_BY_USER`; doğrulanmamış tedarikçi API'leri için endpoint uydurulmadı. 266 Release testi geçti; `Windows-M5-XML` self-contained yayın üretildi.

M6 mağaza ve bağlantı merkezi (2026-09-11, Issue #13): tek WPF panelinde kanal+mağaza metadata, etkinlik, capability, son test/hata ve hızlı geçişler eklendi. Varyant/bundle/hızlı düzenleme/kritik fiyat geliştirilmedi.

M34 API bağlantı sağlığı, kota ve rate-limit merkezi (2026-09-11, Issue #51): Mevcut salt-okunur connector probe'larını `ApiHealthCaptureHandler` ile gözlemleyen `ApiHealthStore` kanal/mağaza bazında auth, HTTP, son başarılı istek, rate-limit/reset, Retry-After, backoff ve maskeli hatayı saklar. 401/403/408/429/5xx, timeout ve ağ hataları sınıflandırılır; backoff aktifken manuel test ertelenir. API bağlantı sağlığı WPF ekranı arama, durum filtresi, sayaç ve bağlantı/kanal geçişlerini sunar. Resmi endpointi doğrulanmayan kanallar `LIVE_API_BLOCKED` kalır; canlı write yoktur. 327 Release testi geçti; self-contained `Windows-M34-Api-Health` yayını üretildi.

M35 otomasyon takvimi, şablonlar ve çalışma pencereleri (2026-09-11, Issue #52): `AutomationStore` mevcut SQLite/lease modelini migration ile genişleterek Interval/Daily/Weekly takvim, yerel saat/gün, çalışma penceresi, retry limiti/backoff, failure count ve template key saklar. XML, stok, fiyat, sağlık ve normal sync şablonları `AutomationRunner` üzerinden yalnız yerel `SyncStore` işine dönüşür; canlı endpoint uydurulmaz. Başarı takvime göre yeni çalışma planlar, hata sınırlı exponential backoff uygular; WPF ekranı manuel due, etkinlik, sonraki çalışma ve audit akışını gösterir. 330 Release testi geçti; self-contained `Windows-M35-Automation-Calendar` yayını üretildi.

Credential/token/password alanları filtrelenir; proprietary DB veya canlı marketplace write yoktur. 336 Release testi geçti; self-contained `Windows-M38-Migration-Assistant` yayını üretildi.

M39 ilk kurulum sihirbazı ve bağlantı onboarding (2026-09-11, Issue #56): `OnboardingStore` son adım/skip/tamamlanma state'ini credential içermeden SQLite'ta saklar; `OnboardingSetupService` mağaza metadata, XML kaynak, stok/fiyat policy ve Excel profil başlangıcını mevcut güvenli store'lara bağlar. İlk açılışta opsiyonel WPF sihirbazı, resume, API capability özeti ve modül hızlı geçişleri sunulur; canlı write yapılmaz. 338 Release testi geçti; self-contained `Windows-M39-Onboarding` yayını üretildi.

M40 son üretim sertleştirmesi (2026-09-11, Issue #57): `ProductionReadinessService` yerel veri klasörü yazılabilirliği, core SQLite okunabilirliği, açık secret biçimleri, veri kalite kritik kayıtları, connector capability sınırı ve API sağlık/backoff durumunu salt-okunur raporlar. `Üretim hazırlığı` WPF ekranı bu geçidi görünür kılar; hiçbir marketplace endpoint'i çağrılmaz ve `LIVE_API_BLOCKED` durumu gizlenmez. Foküslü güvenlik/kalite testleri eklendi; tam Release test ve self-contained publish doğrulaması bu dalın kabul adımıdır.

## #284 Import repair — 2026-09-12

1. **DONE — Mapping/profile revision:** `Catalog/Models.cs`, `Catalog/XmlCatalog.cs`, `Catalog/CatalogStore.cs` ve `MainWindow.xaml.cs` mapping revision, namespace/path/repeat-node fingerprint ve manual/scheduled stale gate’lerini kalıcı kaynak metadata’sına bağlar. `ImportRepairIntegrationTests.XmlMappingSnapshotBlocksNamespaceDriftUntilExplicitRemap` ile namespace drift’in scheduled akışı durdurduğu ve explicit remap sonrası manual akışın açıldığı doğrulandı.
2. **DONE — Source-missing quarantine:** `SourceMissingQuarantine.cs` kalıcı SQLite case/audit store’una dönüştürüldü; `CatalogStore.Import` son başarılı complete feed ile kıyaslayıp eksik SKU’ları grace süresi boyunca WARNING/PENDING_ACTION durumunda tutar, recovery audit’i üretir ve UI source-health özetine yansıtır. `ImportRepairIntegrationTests.XmlImportPersistsMissingSourceGraceAndFeedHashIdempotency` gerçek geçici SQLite ile grace/recovery doğrulamasını yapar.
3. **DONE — Excel entry point:** `MainWindow.Excel.cs` async `CancellationTokenSource`, `ExcelApplyCoordinator`, apply/cancel button state ve re-entry guard kullanır; `CatalogExcel.ApplyWithUndoAsync` preview hash/profile/product revision preflight’inden sonra atomik apply yapar. `SafetyAndStabilityTests.MainWindowExcelPageUsesAsyncApplyCoordinatorAndCancelEntryPoint` gerçek STA/WPF ekranından button/coordinator wiring’ini, `ImportRepairIntegrationTests.ExcelApplyCoordinatorRejectsReentryAndPreservesPreviewMetadata` cancellation/re-entry davranışını doğrular.
4. **DONE — Deterministic culture/parser:** `Catalog/DeterministicNumberParser.cs`, `Catalog/XmlCatalog.cs`, `Catalog/CatalogExcel.cs`, `Catalog/ExcelProfileStore.cs` ve `MigrationAssistant.cs` explicit profile culture kullanır; CurrentCulture/invariant fallback kaldırıldı. Boş, date-like, negatif, ambiguous separator, overflow ve integer olmayan değerler kodlu red sonucu döndürür. `ImportRepairIntegrationTests.DeterministicParserUsesProfileAndReturnsSpecificRejectReasons` tr-TR/en-US ve red sınıflarını doğrular.
5. **DONE — Scheduler restart/idempotency:** `XmlRunStore.cs` lease, heartbeat, abandoned-running recovery ve feed-hash metadata’sı ile SQLite’da kalıcıdır; `CatalogStore.Import` deferred transaction, cancellation ve complete-feed hash replay gate’i ile duplicate mutation’ı engeller. `ImportRepairIntegrationTests.XmlRunStoreRecoversExpiredLeaseAndRejectsConcurrentStart` lease/restart/race, XML import integration testi de replay idempotency’yi doğrular.
6. **DONE — Production-chain performance:** `Catalog/ProductionImportMeasurement.cs` gerçek `XmlSourceReader → MappingSnapshot → Preview → CatalogStore.Import` ve ClosedXML XLSX `Preview → CatalogStore.Import` zincirlerinde elapsed/allocation ölçer; XML yolu ayrıca async yield gözlemi taşır. `ImportRepairIntegrationTests.ProductionXmlAndXlsxChainsProduceBoundedMeasurements` gerçek 120 satırlık XML/XLSX fixture’larında zinciri çalıştırır, import sayaçlarını ve ölçümü doğrular.
7. **DONE — Integration tests:** `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ImportRepairIntegrationTests.cs` gerçek temporary SQLite stores, parser, XML reader/preview/import, XLSX preview ve WPF entry-point testlerini içerir; helper-only tautology kullanılmadı. Release suite sonucu: **106/106 passed**.
8. **DONE — CI/Publish:** `.github/workflows/windows-release-evidence.yml` restore adımı `-r win-x64` ile publish runtime hedefini hazırlar; aynı commit için `dotnet build`, tam Release test, runtime restore ve `dotnet publish ... -r win-x64 --self-contained true` doğrulandı. Yerel artifact: `Windows-Current/TrMarketplaceHubDesktop.exe`. Canlı marketplace write yapılmadı; credential/production connector kanıtı `LIVE_API_BLOCKED` olarak korunuyor.

**Verification commands:** `dotnet build TrMarketplaceHubDesktop.csproj -c Release --no-restore`; `dotnet test tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests.csproj -c Release --no-restore --logger "trx;LogFileName=full-final.trx" --results-directory TestResults` (106/106); `dotnet restore tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests.csproj -r win-x64`; `dotnet publish TrMarketplaceHubDesktop.csproj -c Release -r win-x64 --self-contained true -o Windows-Current --no-restore`; `git diff --check`.

- `ProfitPriceRepairIntegrationTests` covers expense-aware loss/healthy margin, missing inputs and stale FX (targeted: 3/3 passed).

## #287 — dirty product edit repair (2026-09-12)

#287 — scope reconciliation against the issue's own 11-deliverable text (2026-09-12)

Read `gh issue view 287` directly instead of relying on TODO.md summaries. Two of its 11 deliverables are genuinely large, separate pieces of work rather than card-repair-sized fixes, and are recorded here rather than rushed:
- Deliverable 7 (varyant/bundle domain): `VariantDomainDeferredGuard.cs` and `BundleCoreDeferredGuard.cs` are live, intentional guards throwing `InvalidOperationException` for any write — a standing DEFERRED_BY_USER decision. Dedicated issues #249 ("Ortak varyant ve seçenek domain çekirdeği") and #253 ("Paket, set ve bundle ürün çekirdeği") already exist, both OPEN, for building these domains properly. Building a full variant/option/bundle core inside this card-repair issue would both reverse a standing user decision unilaterally and duplicate #249/#253's scope; left to those issues.
- Deliverable 5 (#285 profit/margin source): confirmed `MoneyPriceCalculator.Calculate` (the #285 expense-aware calculator) has zero production callers anywhere in the app — only its own tests call it. `CatalogProduct.ApproximateProfit`/`ApproximateMarginPercent` (used by both the list and the card) are a naive `Price - Cost`, not wired to it. Wiring it correctly needs a new per-channel-shop settings surface for commission rate, shipping estimate, transaction cost and an FX rate snapshot — none of which has any existing storage or UI in this app (`PricePolicy` only carries `TryPerUnit`/`MinimumPrice`/`MinimumMarginTry`). This is a new subsystem, not a quick rewire; attempting it without that settings UI would make every product row report `BlockedMissingInput`. Left open, documented here rather than half-wired.
- Deliverable 9 (list↔card selection/filter/page preservation): the app's actual architecture is a single persistent split view (`Split(Dock(bar,products),BuildProductWorkspace(),350)`) — there is no separate list screen to navigate away from and back to. `productOffset`/`productFilter`/`search.Text` are MainWindow instance fields reused by every `RefreshProducts()` call, and `CancelReplacingPageKeepsExistingItemsAndSelection` already covers page/selection survival across a `ShowProducts` replacement. This deliverable's premise (a separate list-vs-card navigation) does not match the implemented UI; no separate-screen round-trip exists to test.
No production code was changed in this entry; it is a scope/status reconciliation so the next repair pass does not re-investigate the same three points from zero.

## #288 — deliverable 4: Navlungo callback protections (2026-09-12)

Fetched Navlungo's actual official public docs (github.com/Navlungo/public-api-docs, fetched 2026-09-12 — not the "report" this issue warns against trusting blindly) to verify the OAuth authorization_code flow before touching anything:
- README.md §2.1 confirms the code_challenge is standard `Convert.ToBase64String(SHA256(verifier))`, NOT Base64Url — the existing code comment claiming this was already correct.
- The authorize request only requires `client_id` and `code_challenge`; there is no documented `response_type`/`scope`/`redirect_uri` parameter, so the existing `Begin()` URL was not missing anything required.
- The docs state Navlungo echoes back *every* querystring parameter the client originally sent to `/authorize`, including any extras like `state` — so the existing `state` CSRF parameter, while not a Navlungo-documented field, is safe to rely on because of this echo behavior.
- token.md documents the Token API (`POST v1/oauth/token`) requiring `client_id`, `client_secret`, `grant_type`, `code`, `code_verifier`, and a `scope` that "en az `openid offline_access`" for the authorization_code flow.

Real gap found: there was no callback handler at all — only the authorize-URL builder (`Begin`) existed; nothing validated a returned callback or prepared a Token API request. Added `NavlungoConnection.HandleCallback(settings, pending, callbackQuery)`, which rejects: wrong `state` (CSRF), a `client_id` in the callback that doesn't match the settings that started the flow (wrong-account), a missing/blank `code`, and replay of an already-consumed `NavlungoAuthorizationPreparation` (a new `Consumed` flag rejects a second call with the same pending object). On success it returns a `NavlungoTokenRequest` with the documented minimum `openid offline_access` scope. No HTTP call is made; `LIVE_API_BLOCKED` is unchanged — this only adds the safe, testable protection layer the callback entry point will need.
Added `InternalsVisibleTo` for the test project (`TrMarketplaceHubDesktop.csproj`) so tests can exercise `NavlungoAuthorizationPreparation`'s internal `State`/`Verifier` without weakening the public API surface.
`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NavlungoConnectionTests.cs` (new, 6 tests): valid callback produces the correct token request; wrong state, mismatched client_id, missing code, and replay are each independently rejected; the code_challenge is verified byte-for-byte against the official Base64(SHA256(verifier)) formula.
REAL_WORK_COUNT=1 (built the missing callback protection layer, doc-verified); FILES_CHANGED=NavlungoConnection.cs, TrMarketplaceHubDesktop.csproj, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NavlungoConnectionTests.cs, IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=140 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 134); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish (`publish/navlungo-callback-verify`) succeeded; artifact SHA-256 fac801779c5e379e68fd014c7129054be51fff0254dd529d379860f8fa60ca39.
Not done yet: wiring `HandleCallback`/`HandleCallback`'s output into an actual browser-redirect listener and the Token API HTTP call (both still LIVE_API_BLOCKED/no UI entry point — NavlungoPanel.cs explicitly states token exchange is not enabled in this version); Etsy webhook/message/return capability re-classification (deliverable 5) and the NES/FAST invoice mapping review (deliverable 6) remain open.

## #288 — deliverable 5: Etsy webhook/message/return capability classification (2026-09-12)

`EtsyCapabilityAudit.OfficialManifest` had no entries at all for webhook, message or return capabilities — deliverable 1's required WORKING/PARTIAL/UNSUPPORTED/UNVERIFIED classification simply didn't exist for these three areas. Fetched Etsy's real current docs instead of assuming either "yok" or "var":
- Webhooks: https://developers.etsy.com/documentation/essentials/webhooks/ (fetched 2026-09-12) — only four events exist today (`order.paid`, `order.canceled`, `order.shipped`, `order.delivered`), available to both commercial and personal apps, subscribed through Etsy's Webhook Portal UI (no documented REST subscribe endpoint). Classified **PARTIAL**.
- Messages: no conversations/messaging endpoint exists in Etsy Open API v3 at all (confirmed via the official reference and community discussion etsy/open-api#1547 requesting it as missing). Classified **UNSUPPORTED**.
- Returns/refunds: https://developer.etsy.com/documentation/tutorials/payments (fetched 2026-09-12) — quoted verbatim: "The Open API v3 endpoints for payments and the shop ledger are read-only operations ... refunds are not automatic." No refund-creation endpoint exists. Classified **UNSUPPORTED**.

Added all three to `OfficialManifest` with source citations, and widened `MissingOrBlocked()` to also surface `UNSUPPORTED`.

**Regression caught and fixed before commit:** the new entries' non-scope `Scope` values (e.g. "n/a") were being pulled into `EtsyOAuthReadiness.Evaluate`'s required-scope set, which iterates `OfficialManifest` for every non-BLOCKED entry — this flipped `EtsySellerCockpitAggregatesReadinessAndKeepsDryRunSeparate` from READY_READ_ONLY to ATTENTION because the fully-scoped test app now looked like it was missing scope "n/a". Fixed by also excluding UNSUPPORTED/PARTIAL entries from that aggregation (their Scope field is a documentation placeholder, not a grantable OAuth scope), and added a regression test (`ready.MissingScopes` must never contain "n/a") so this can't silently regress again.
REAL_WORK_COUNT=1 (found and fixed a real regression before it shipped, plus filled the 3-capability classification gap); FILES_CHANGED=EtsyCapabilityAudit.cs, EtsyOAuthReadiness.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SafetyAndStabilityTests.cs, IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=141 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 140, after catching and fixing the regression — never shipped broken); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish (`publish/etsy-capability-verify`) succeeded; artifact SHA-256 20986d7f112909d30d76ec9e3ef068e0851364e8c9ffc0e5ea44041d3cd01906.

## #307 — scheduler decouple: stock/price automation was silently skipped whenever no XML source was due (2026-09-12)

Verified the reported bug by actually constructing and running `MainWindow.ScheduledAsync()` in a real Windows STA test (not a static-code read): `if(due.Count==0)return;` at the top of the scheduled tick returned from the ENTIRE method before the AutomationStore (Stock/Price/Health/Sync) job loop ever ran. Any user with no XML sources configured, all XML sources disabled, or simply between XML intervals would have their due Stock/Price automation jobs silently never execute — this is the exact "stok/fiyat takibi" reliability the product depends on.

Root-caused with a red/green TDD cycle: wrote `SchedulerDecoupleTests.cs`, confirmed it failed against the original code, then fixed `ScheduledAsync` to compute `dueJobs` independently of `due` and only skip the tick when BOTH are empty (`if(due.Count==0&&dueJobs.Count==0)return;`), preserving every other line of the original XML-import and automation-dispatch bodies unchanged.

**Note on PR_BASE:** issue #307 names `codex/issue-278-ci-enforcement` as PR_BASE. Checked that branch directly and found a real, independent, pre-existing crash there: `productChannelSummary` is added as a child to both the "Genel" tab's `productEditor` panel and the "Pazaryerleri" tab's panel in the same `BuildProductWorkspace()` pass, which WPF rejects ("already the logical child of another element") — this is the exact double-parent defect later fixed by the `productChannelSummaryMirror` split in the #283→#287 lineage. Since #278 is a strictly older, already-superseded checkpoint whose known defects are already fixed further down the real active chain, this repair was branched from `codex/issue-288-connector-contract-repair` (the actual current safe checkpoint) instead of resurrecting #278, per the project's own "no stale-branch fallback" rule.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SchedulerDecoupleTests.cs` (new, 3 tests, all constructing a real `MainWindow` and invoking the real private `ScheduledAsync` entry point via reflection with a pumped `DispatcherFrame`, not a helper-only unit test): a due Price automation job runs with zero due XML sources; nothing runs when neither is due; a job whose `NextRunUtc` advances after running is not re-run on an immediate second tick.
REAL_WORK_COUNT=1 (root-caused and fixed a real reliability bug via TDD, plus found and documented an unrelated pre-existing crash on the issue's stated PR_BASE); FILES_CHANGED=MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SchedulerDecoupleTests.cs, IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=144 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 141); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish (`publish/scheduler-decouple-verify`) succeeded; artifact SHA-256 8b715b393a99cfdfe13753647b1b38996b6886749c95a88a89314b6234cd053e.
Remaining #307 deliverables not done: extracting the tick body into a separately-testable scheduler service (deliverable 3 only partially satisfied — I test the real entry point directly instead), explicit UI text distinguishing "tick ran" from "job executed" (deliverable 6), and #293 lease/shutdown edge-case regression coverage (deliverable 5) beyond what already existed.

## #307 follow-up — hardened test cleanup against a shared fixture-cleanup race (2026-09-12)

The CI run for the previous commit failed: all 3 new `SchedulerDecoupleTests` failed in `Dispose()` with `source-health.db`/`catalog.db` "used by another process". Root-caused rather than assumed a flake: seeding an `XmlSource` and letting `MainWindow` construct auto-selects it (`RefreshSources()` sets `sources.SelectedIndex=0`), which fires the same `_ = RefreshXmlSourceHealthAsync(s)` fire-and-forget call used elsewhere in the app — its `SourceMissingQuarantine`/health SQLite connection can still be closing when `Dispose()` starts. This is a *shared* fixture-cleanup race, not specific to my test: it is the same failure signature already seen once on `ProductCardDirtyDraftTests.SaveDuringRefreshReloadsTheSavedVersion`, which also seeds an `XmlSource`.
Hardened `SchedulerDecoupleTests.Fixture.Dispose()` to re-call `SqliteConnection.ClearAllPools()` immediately before every delete attempt (not just once), since a connection opened after the first clear would otherwise never be released within the retry budget. Verified 3 consecutive full local runs green after the fix (previously reproduced the failure locally too, confirming it wasn't CI-only).
FILES_CHANGED=tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SchedulerDecoupleTests.cs, IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=144 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 3 consecutive local runs; Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish (`publish/scheduler-decouple-verify2`) succeeded; artifact SHA-256 70d89878d7c02f2d201b441938bc454b9e41ef9e8cf2790c787570be2c5e7e87.
Note for future test authors: any fixture that seeds an `XmlSource` via `store.Import` is exposed to this same background-health-check cleanup race; the shared fix pattern here (re-clear pools per delete attempt) is the safe default until the app itself awaits/cancels that background task in a disposable lifetime scope.

## #320 — multiple independent price fields (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-320-multi-price-fields off codex/issue-307-scheduler-decouple

Added `CatalogPriceField(Name, Value, Currency)` and a `PriceFields` list on `CatalogProduct` (Catalog/Models.cs). Since products are stored as a JSON blob column (`CatalogProducts.Json`, no relational schema), old records deserialize with an empty `PriceFields` list automatically — no migration script needed; verified with a dedicated test that a pre-existing product's `Price`/`Cost` are unchanged and `PriceFields` is empty after reload.

`Catalog/ProductPriceFields.cs` (new): `AddPriceField`/`UpdatePriceField`/`RemovePriceField` on `CatalogStore`, built on the existing `FindProduct`/`SaveProduct` optimistic-concurrency path rather than duplicating it. `AddPriceField` rejects a case-insensitive duplicate name; `UpdatePriceField`/`RemovePriceField` require the name to already exist.

`Catalog/PricePolicy.cs`: added `PricePolicy.PriceFieldName` (nullable). `PreviewPrice` now checks it first — when set, it looks up that named field on the product, requires its `Currency` to match the policy's `Currency` (rejects a silent unit mismatch instead of producing a wrong number), applies the existing `MinimumPrice` guard, and returns its value directly with **no formula evaluation**. When unset, the original formula path runs completely unchanged (backward compatible; existing formula-only channels are unaffected). A missing/misspelled field name throws a clear exception naming the field instead of producing a silent 0/null price.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/MultiPriceFieldsTests.cs` (new, 6 tests): field-selected preview overrides the formula; no-selection preview still uses the formula (legacy behavior); a legacy product without `PriceFields` keeps its `Price`/`Cost` after reload; a policy pointing at a nonexistent field name throws (never silently 0); duplicate field names are rejected on add; update/remove require an existing name.

## #319 — desi field + shipping cost table + auto profit integration (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-319-desi-shipping-table off codex/issue-320-multi-price-fields

Added `CatalogProduct.Desi` (nullable decimal, Catalog/Models.cs). Same JSON-blob storage as #320: verified with a migration test that a pre-existing product without `Desi` reloads with `Desi == null` (never a silently-assumed 0), `Price`/`Cost` unchanged.

New `Catalog/ShippingCostTable.cs`: `ShippingCostBracket(Carrier, DesiFrom, DesiTo, Cost, Currency)`, half-open `[DesiFrom, DesiTo)` bracket resolution per carrier, rejects overlapping brackets for the same carrier and invalid ranges/currency on add.

`ProfitabilitySimulator.cs`: added `ResolveShippingCost(table, carrier, desi)` returning an explicit `AutoShippingCost(Cost, Status)` — `NEEDS_WEIGHT_DATA` when `desi` is null, `SHIPPING_COST_UNKNOWN` when the desi falls outside every bracket for that carrier, `OK` with the resolved cost otherwise — and `SimulateAuto(...)`, a new entry point that resolves shipping automatically and short-circuits to the explicit status instead of ever calling `Simulate` with an assumed-zero shipping cost. The original `Simulate(ProfitabilityInput, ...)` overload and every existing caller are unchanged.

**Material omission found and not implemented, per this task's own instruction to report rather than force it:** the issue's premise that `ChannelFeeCatalog.cs` "kargo maliyetini parametre olarak dışarıdan bekliyor" doesn't match the actual code — read the full file; `ChannelFeeCatalog` only models channel commission fees (`CommissionPercent`/`FixedFee`), and has no shipping-related field or method at all. Wiring shipping auto-derivation into it would bolt an unrelated concern onto a cleanly-scoped commission class. Left untouched; the real (and only) external-shipping-cost seam is `ProfitabilityInput.ShippingCost`, which `SimulateAuto` now covers.

## #321 — XML product feed export (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-321-xml-product-export off codex/issue-319-desi-shipping-table

Confirmed #284 (its stated blocker) is functionally complete before starting (TODO.md marks it "tamamlandı"; not BLOCKED anywhere). New `Catalog/XmlCatalogExporter.cs`:
- `Filter(products, XmlExportFilter)`: category/brand/sourceId filtering.
- `XmlExportTemplate`/`XmlExportField(Property, Tag, Formula?)`: configurable field→tag mapping; `StandardTemplate` ships as the one built-in default (Sku/Barcode/Name/Description/Brand/Category/Cost/Stock).
- `Export(products, template, cancellationToken)`: validates every field maps to a real `CatalogProduct` property and every formula compiles *before* writing anything (fails fast, never produces a half-written or silently-empty document); reuses `PriceFormula.Evaluate` for formula-derived fields (e.g. cost marked up 20% on export) — no new formula engine. Checks the cancellation token every product, proven with a lazy source that cancels mid-stream and asserts the exporter stopped early rather than draining a 20k-item enumerable first.
- `ExportAsync(...)`: `Task.Run` wrapper so a UI caller awaits instead of blocking, per the issue's "senkron değil" requirement.
- `StandardRoundTripSource(id)`: a ready `XmlSource` (PriceMode=Simple, matching Fields) so the standard template's own output can be read back through the *existing* `XmlCatalog.Preview` import pipeline — no bespoke re-reader.

`AutomationRunner`/`AutomationStore`: added `AutomationKind.XmlExport`, routed alongside Xml/Health/Sync to enqueue a `"xml-export"` `SyncRequest` when a due export job is claimed — reuses the existing automation→sync-queue architecture (the same pattern `Xml`/`Health` already use) rather than adding a parallel execution path.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/XmlCatalogExporterTests.cs` (new, 9 tests): valid XML for a filtered set; custom field-mapping renames tags and omits unmapped fields; formula-derived field value; round-trip re-import via `XmlCatalog.Preview` matches product count and SKUs; a due `XmlExport` automation job is actually queued through `AutomationRunner.RunDue` (not just theoretically wired); mapping to a nonexistent property throws instead of producing empty XML; an empty template throws instead of producing empty XML; cancellation stops a 20k-item export mid-stream (deterministic, not timing-based); `ExportAsync` completes correctly for 15k items.

## #319/#320/#321 UI wiring — desi field, price fields panel (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-319-320-321-ui-wiring off codex/issue-321-xml-product-export

Closes the "Not done: UI panel" gaps left open by #319 and #320 (product-card side only; #321's export-trigger UI remains open, see below).

`MainWindow.xaml.cs` `BuildProducts()`: added a "Desi (kargo)" field bound two-way to `CatalogProduct.Desi` next to the existing VatRate field. Added `BuildPriceFieldsPanel(productEditor)`: renders the current product's `PriceFields` list (name/value/currency, one row per field with a "Sil" delete button) plus an add-row (name/value/currency text boxes + "Fiyat alanı ekle" button) that calls the existing `store.AddPriceField`/`store.RemovePriceField` (from #320) — no new domain logic, this is UI-only wiring onto the already-tested store methods. `RefreshPriceFieldsPanel()` is called from `BindProductEdit` (MainWindow.ProductEditing.cs) so the list follows product selection like every other field.

**Root-caused and fixed a test-authoring bug that hung the test host indefinitely, not a production bug:** `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductCardPriceFieldsUiTests.cs`'s first version set `desi.Text = "2.5"` and asserted the parsed decimal — under this machine's current culture, the WPF binding parses `.` as a group separator (not a decimal point), so "2.5" silently became `25`, failing the assertion. That `AssertFailedException`, thrown from inside `using var f = new Fixture()`, triggered `Fixture.Dispose()` -> `Window.Close()` -> `MainWindow.OnClosing` -> `ResolveProductEdit()` (the product edit was dirty), which opened the real "kaydedilmemiş değişiklikler" modal dialog via `ShowDialog()` — a nested WPF message pump with nothing to click, since (unlike every other UI test fixture in this suite, e.g. `ProductCardDirtyDraftTests`) this new fixture's `Dispose()` had no auto-dismiss for that dialog. The test host hung for 90+ minutes (confirmed via `dotnet-dump` thread stacks: the STA thread was parked in `Dispatcher.PushFrameImpl` -> `GetMessage` inside `AskProductEditDecision`'s `ShowDialog()`) instead of ever reporting a result. Fixed both root causes: (1) the test now builds its input via `2.5m.ToString(CultureInfo.CurrentCulture)` so it matches whatever culture the binding actually parses with, and (2) `Fixture.Dispose()` now schedules the same auto-dismiss-any-unsaved-changes-dialog pattern already used elsewhere in this suite, so a future assertion failure fails fast instead of hanging CI.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductCardPriceFieldsUiTests.cs` (new, 2 tests): `DesiFieldBindsToSelectedProduct` (TextBox -> `CatalogProduct.Desi` two-way binding), `AddingAndRemovingAPriceFieldUpdatesTheListAndThePersistedProduct` (add via UI persists through `CatalogStore.FindProduct` and appears in the list; remove via UI clears both).

## #285 — expense-aware money preflight wired into the real price-dispatch path (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-285-money-price-repair off codex/issue-319-320-321-ui-wiring

PARTIAL, not full closure of #285's 8 deliverables — see "Not done" below. This pass fixes the specific, most-recently-audited BLOCKED finding (MARKETPLACE_ZIP_AUDIT_2026_09_12): `AutomationRunner.RunClaimed -> catalog.PreviewPrice` computed its minimum-margin gate as `formula - product.Cost < MinimumMarginTry` — a naive gross diff with **no commission, shipping, or tax awareness at all**, and `PricePolicy.Enabled` was never even checked. The already-built-but-unwired `MoneyPriceCalculator`/`MoneyPriceInput`/`PriceDispatchPreflight` (from an earlier attempt, previously only unit-tested standalone per the CURRENT-SHA and FORWARD_REPAIR_V2 audit comments on this issue) is now the actual gate on this real dispatch path.

**Exact audit fixture verified against the real path, not the standalone calculator:** cost=100 TRY, sale=130 TRY (formula-derived), commission=20% of sale, shipping=10 TRY → real contribution `130-100-26-10=-6 TRY` (loss) → `AutomationRunner.RunDue` on the real `AutomationJob`/`PricePolicy`/`CatalogProduct` now returns `Queued=0` and the created sync row is `Failed` (never reaches `Pending`, so a live dispatcher can never pick it up), even though the naive old check (`+30 TRY` gross diff, well above any positive `MinimumMarginTry`) would have allowed it. Positive control sale=160 TRY → contribution `+18 TRY` → allowed, `Queued=1`, sync row `Pending`.

`Catalog/PricePolicy.cs`: added `CommissionPercent`, `EstimatedShippingTry`, `TransactionCostTry`, `VatRatePercent`, `VatIncludedInSale`, `FxRateObservedUtc` (all nullable/default — a policy saved before this change deserializes with these null, per JSON-blob storage, and now correctly **blocks** instead of silently treating unknown commission/shipping/tax as zero). `PreviewPrice` now: (1) rejects a disabled policy outright (previously never checked), (2) rejects a product whose `CostCurrency` isn't TRY instead of silently treating it as TRY (the shared calculator has no cost-side FX leg), (3) builds a `MoneyPriceInput` from the policy's expense fields + the computed sale price (from either the `PriceFieldName` path or the `Formula` path — both paths, unified) and calls `MoneyPriceCalculator.Calculate` + `PriceDispatchPreflight.EnsureReady`, replacing the naive check. A TRY-currency policy needs no FX snapshot (there's no FX leg); a non-TRY policy's `FxRateObservedUtc` is required and checked for staleness by the existing (unmodified) calculator's 24h window.

Because `PricePolicy.PreviewPrice` is the one method both the automation path (`AutomationRunner.RunClaimed`) and the manual UI path (`MainWindow.PricePolicies` "Kayıtlı kuralla önizle") call, fixing it here fixes both real dispatch entries at once — no second gate was written.

`MainWindow.PricePolicies.cs`: added Commission/Shipping/TransactionCost/VatRate/VatIncluded inputs, an Enabled checkbox, and — finally used — the pre-existing "Kur gözlem tarihi" input (previously parsed into a local `time` variable that was silently discarded and never saved; now saved as `FxRateObservedUtc`).

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/MoneyAwarePriceDispatchTests.cs` (new, 6 tests, all driving the real `AutomationRunner.RunDue`/`CatalogStore.PreviewPrice` path, not the standalone calculator): the audit's loss fixture is blocked; the positive-control healthy margin is allowed; a disabled policy blocks; missing commission/shipping/VAT blocks instead of assuming zero; a stale (>24h) FX observation on a non-TRY policy blocks; a foreign `CostCurrency` blocks instead of being silently treated as TRY. (Two pre-existing #320 tests — `MultiPriceFieldsTests.PricePolicy_UsesSelectedPriceFieldWhenConfigured`/`_FallsBackToLegacyPriceWhenNoFieldSelected` — needed explicit zero-value expense fields added since they predate this gate and were testing field-selection, not money correctness; both still assert their original price/currency outcome unchanged.)

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=Catalog/PricePolicy.cs, MainWindow.PricePolicies.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/MoneyAwarePriceDispatchTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/MultiPriceFieldsTests.cs, IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=176 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 170); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 da1aa0a38f09a9a1ac91adf7a5fbc916a7fd300d1a229cf60df415a919a55dad.

**Not done (honest gap against #285's full 8-item scope):**
- **XML import price path** (`Catalog/XmlCatalog.cs -> Preview -> CatalogPricing.Create(source)`) still does not route through the money preflight — it sets `CatalogProduct.Price` from the source's own formula/currency config at import time, a genuinely different concern (populating a local list price from supplier data) from gating a live marketplace price *submission*. Left unconnected; flagged rather than forced, since wiring it would require deciding whether an import-time list price should be blockable by a per-channel/shop policy it doesn't yet know at import time.
- **FX rate caching/single-flight per source+pair+date** (deliverable #4) was not built; `TryPerUnit`/`FxRateObservedUtc` remain per-policy manual values, matching the existing UI's pre-#285 model (a "Kuru şimdi güncelle" auto-FX button exists only for XML sources, not price policies).
- **No persisted "approval" object to go stale**: this codebase's `PreviewPrice` has never cached a prior preview to invalidate — every call recomputes fresh from current policy+product state. This structurally avoids ever *reusing* a stale approval, but it also means "approval staleness on change" (deliverable #5) has no explicit test of a change invalidating a *previously shown* preview, since nothing is retained to invalidate.
- **Common calculation DTO shared with product list/card/report** (deliverable #6, and #286/#287/#289) not built — out of scope for this pass.
- **ChannelFeeCatalog** (the audited, provenance-tracked, date-ranged fee source suggested by the original PDF finding) was deliberately NOT used as the fee source here: it has no persistence and nothing populates it anywhere in the app. Extending the already-persisted, already-real-dispatch-connected `PricePolicy` with the missing expense fields was the surgical fix that didn't require inventing a new subsystem; this is a design choice, not an oversight, and is recorded here so a future pass can decide whether `ChannelFeeCatalog` should replace these per-policy fields once it has a store and an import UI of its own.

## #276 re-verification — Etsy currency mismatch, zero-stock deactivation, OAuth single-flight (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-276-etsy-live-safety-reverify off codex/issue-285-money-price-repair

This issue explicitly requires re-verifying each of its three source claims *on the current SHA with a test* before fixing anything ("Bu tur tam kod audit'i tekrarlanmadı... zaten düzelmişse aynı davranışı tekrar yazma"). All three claims were re-verified by reading the current code; two were confirmed already fixed and previously untested at the real-dispatch level (now proven with new tests, no production code changed for those two); the third is a real, separate testability gap (see below), left unfixed with the reason recorded.

**Claim 1 — remote shop currency not validated / LinkListing allows a wrong currency mapping: ALREADY FIXED, now proven.** `EtsyShopClient.ValidateShopCurrencyAsync` does call the real Etsy shop endpoint (`GET /v3/application/shops/{id}`) and compares its `currency_code` against the expected currency — not a locally-copied preview, a real remote call. `EtsyListingSyncService.DispatchAsync` calls it *before* `UpdateSimpleListingAsync`. `EtsyLiveSafetyGateBlocksUnknownOrMismatchedCurrency` (pre-existing) only tested the standalone `EtsyLiveSafetyGate.ValidateCurrency` helper, which never makes an HTTP call — it could not prove the real path sends zero writes on mismatch. New test `EtsyLiveDispatchSafetyTests.DispatchBlocksOnRemoteShopCurrencyMismatchAndSendsZeroPatchRequests` drives the actual `DispatchAsync` with a fake shop-currency response that disagrees with the product's currency and asserts exactly one HTTP request was made (the GET) and zero PATCH requests — i.e. the mismatch is caught before any write, on the real path.

**Claim 2 — quantity<=0 hard rejection blocks stopping a sale when stock depletes: ALREADY FIXED, now proven.** `EtsyListingSyncService.CreatePreview` only rejects `Stock<0` (zero is valid); `EtsyShopClient.UpdateSimpleListingAsync` only rejects `quantity<0` and sets `state=inactive` in the same PATCH when `quantity==0` — a deactivation plan, not a hard reject. New tests `DispatchSendsExactQuantityAndPriceWhenCurrencyMatches` (healthy stock: exact quantity/price PATCH body, no `state` field touched) and `DispatchDeactivatesTheListingInsteadOfHardRejectingWhenStockReachesZero` (stock=0: preview builds successfully, `DeactivatesListing==true`, and the PATCH body actually contains both `quantity=0` and `state=inactive`) prove this end-to-end.

**Claim 3 — OAuth refresh calls can race: PARTIALLY re-verified, left untested by design.** `MainWindow.AuthorizedAsync()` (the only two production call sites of a Etsy-credential refresh are `MainWindow.Automation.cs` and one other `MainWindow.xaml.cs` helper, both routing through this one method) already serializes on a single `authorizationGate` SemaphoreSlim(1,1) and re-reads the persisted credential *after* acquiring the lock before deciding to call `RefreshAsync`, so a second caller that loses the race sees the first caller's already-refreshed, already-saved credential instead of firing a second refresh. `CredentialStore.Save` writes atomically (temp file + `File.Move(..., true)`). This app has exactly one Etsy shop/credential slot (`CredentialStore` is a single fixed-path file, not shop-keyed), so the issue's "one shop's lock must not block another shop" requirement does not apply to this architecture. A separate, never-called `EtsyOAuth.RefreshIfNeededAsync` method genuinely has no locking at all — but it is dead code (zero call sites found anywhere in the app), not a reachable race.

**Not tested, and why — a real gap, not an oversight:** `CredentialStore`'s path is hardcoded to `%LocalAppData%\MonoBridgeDesktop\credentials.bin` (Windows DPAPI CurrentUser-protected), with no test-root override parameter, unlike every other store in this codebase (`CatalogStore(dataDirectory)`, `AutomationStore(dataDirectory)`, etc.). Driving `AuthorizedAsync()`/`CredentialStore` from an automated test on this machine would read and overwrite this machine's real stored Etsy OAuth credentials (this app is in real use for actual dropshipping work) — an unacceptable risk to take just to prove a concurrency claim. Recorded here rather than worked around: a future pass should make `CredentialStore`'s path injectable (matching the `dataDirectory` convention everywhere else) before this specific claim can be safely tested by automation.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/EtsyLiveDispatchSafetyTests.cs` (new, 3 tests, all against the real `EtsyListingSyncService`/`EtsyShopClient` with a fake `HttpMessageHandler`, no real network, no real credentials file).

**Not done (honest gap against #276's full 12-item scope):** this pass only re-verified and proved the three originally-cited claims. It does NOT cover: full call-path mapping of every price/link/draft/automation write (deliverable #1), official-contract-dated currency source documentation (#2's "belge tarihini yaz"), stale-approval invalidation on shop/currency/version change beyond what `DispatchAsync`'s existing equality check already does (#5 — not independently tested here), cross-process refresh coordination or single-instance guard (#7), separate deactivation-vs-reactivation approval flows and reconciliation on ambiguous remote state (#9), the full fake-HTTP scenario matrix (#10 lists ~9 named scenarios; only 3 are covered here), and the concurrency-barrier test for simultaneous same-account refreshes (#11 — blocked by the `CredentialStore` path issue above). #276 remains open.

## CredentialStore testability fix — follow-up to #276's flagged gap (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-276-etsy-live-safety-reverify

Closes the specific gap #276's re-verification pass flagged rather than worked around: `CredentialStore.Save`/`Load` had no directory parameter at all, unlike every other store in this codebase, and always resolved to the one real `%LocalAppData%\MonoBridgeDesktop\credentials.bin` file. That made it unsafe to exercise from an automated test on this machine (real, currently-in-use Etsy OAuth credentials live there).

`CredentialStore.cs`: `Save`/`Load` now take an optional `string? directory = null`, defaulting to the exact same real path as before (zero behavior change for the running app) — mirrors the established `TemplateStore.Load(string? directory = null)` convention exactly. `MainWindow.xaml.cs`: both call sites now pass `dataDirectory` (the field every other store already uses) — the constructor's credential-load previously had a `directory==null?CredentialStore.Load():null` special case that *skipped loading entirely* for a test-constructed window; it now loads through the test's own isolated directory instead, and `SetAndSave` (the method `AuthorizedAsync`'s refresh path calls) is no longer able to write to the real file from a test. `EtsyReadiness.cs` had the identical unparameterized `CredentialStore.Load()` call despite already holding a per-instance `directory` field used one line later for `TemplateStore.Load(directory)` — fixed the same way. (`MarketplaceConnectionsPanel.cs`'s `ProbeAsync` has one more unparameterized `CredentialStore.Load()` call; left untouched since it's a static helper with no directory context of its own and no test currently reaches it — noted here rather than expanding scope to thread a directory through that panel's whole call graph.)

This did **not** fully unlock testing `AuthorizedAsync`'s OAuth single-flight refresh end-to-end as originally hoped: `MainWindow` holds its own internal `HttpClient` field with no injection seam, so `AuthorizedAsync`'s `EtsyOAuth(http).RefreshAsync(...)` call would still hit the real Etsy endpoint if a test triggered it. That is a separate, larger refactor (injectable `HttpClient` on `MainWindow`) not attempted here.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/CredentialStoreTests.cs` (new, 4 tests): load-returns-null-before-any-save, full round-trip of every field, two directories fully isolated from each other, and overwrite-on-second-save — none of which touch the real credentials file.

## #276 claim 3 closed with a real test — MainWindow HttpClient made injectable (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-276-etsy-live-safety-reverify

Closes the one remaining gap from #276's re-verification pass: `MainWindow.AuthorizedAsync`'s OAuth single-flight refresh was previously "verified by code reading only" because `MainWindow` held its own internal `HttpClient` with no way to substitute a fake handler in a test.

`MainWindow.xaml.cs`: the `http` field is no longer a field initializer; the constructor gained an optional `HttpClient? httpClient = null` parameter (`public MainWindow(string? directory, HttpClient? httpClient = null)`), defaulting to the exact same real client construction as before when omitted — zero behavior change for the running app and every existing test/call site (`new MainWindow(root)` still compiles and behaves identically). Verified no other field initializer referenced `http` before the constructor body would have assigned it.

With that plus the earlier `CredentialStore(directory)` fix, `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/EtsyOAuthSingleFlightTests.cs` (new, 1 test) now drives the real production path end-to-end: seeds an expired credential with a refresh token via `CredentialStore.Save` into a test root, constructs `MainWindow` with a fake token-endpoint `HttpMessageHandler` that deliberately delays 150ms before responding (a real synchronization race, not a timing hope — the delay widens the window deterministically so the second caller reaches the semaphore before the first's refresh resolves), fires two concurrent `AuthorizedAsync()` calls via reflection (pumped through a `DispatcherSynchronizationContext` + `DispatcherFrame`, the same pattern established in `SchedulerDecoupleTests`), and asserts exactly one real POST reached the fake token endpoint while both callers still received the freshly-refreshed token. This is genuine proof, not a rewrite: no production locking logic changed, since `AuthorizedAsync`'s existing `authorizationGate` semaphore + re-read-after-lock pattern was already correct — the test simply became possible to write.

## Flaky CI fix — ProductCardDirtyDraftTests.Fixture.Dispose() source-health.db race (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-276-etsy-live-safety-reverify

Not related to #276's own code: the previous push's CI run failed with `IOException: The process cannot access the file 'source-health.db'...` in `ProductCardDirtyDraftTests.SelectionRequiresExplicitDecision`/`CancelReplacingPageKeepsExistingItemsAndSelection`'s `Fixture.Dispose()`. This is the same pre-existing, shared race documented earlier in this session for #307's `SchedulerDecoupleTests`: any fixture that seeds an `XmlSource` causes `MainWindow`'s constructor to auto-select it, firing a fire-and-forget `RefreshXmlSourceHealthAsync` whose `source-health.db` connection can still be closing when `Directory.Delete` runs. Confirmed local-only-stable (3/3 clean runs of this file alone) but flaky under full-suite I/O pressure, matching the earlier diagnosis exactly — not a genuine regression from this session's other changes.

Applied the same fix already used for `SchedulerDecoupleTests`: `Fixture.Dispose()` now calls `SqliteConnection.ClearAllPools()` before *every* retry attempt (not once), retrying `Directory.Delete` up to 30 times with a 300ms backoff on `IOException`/`UnauthorizedAccessException`.

The previous push's CI run still failed, this time in `ProductCardPriceFieldsUiTests.Fixture.Dispose()` -- the same root cause, a different fixture. Rather than keep patching them one at a time as CI happens to surface them, searched every test file in the suite for the vulnerable pattern (`new MainWindow(root)` constructed after an `XmlSource` was already imported into that root, whose auto-select on construction fires the fire-and-forget health check that can still be closing `source-health.db` when `Directory.Delete` runs). Found and fixed three more: `ProductCardPriceFieldsUiTests.cs`, `ProductCardKeyboardSmokeTests.cs`, `ProductOrderReportIntegrationTests.cs` -- all now use the same per-attempt-`ClearAllPools()` retry loop already applied to `SchedulerDecoupleTests` and `ProductCardDirtyDraftTests`. ## #692 — XML source URL reachability and latency health check (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-692-xml-source-health off codex/issue-276-etsy-live-safety-reverify

New issue, no prior audit history; found the exact gap it names by reading the existing code: `MainWindow`'s existing `RefreshXmlSourceHealthAsync` only summarizes *local* state (product count, last import run, quarantine counts) with no network call at all, and the one method that does make a real request (`CheckXmlSourceAsync`, the "Kaynak sağlığını kontrol et" button) is manual-only, measures no latency, classifies every failure through one generic catch-all message, and persists nothing. Separately, `AutomationKind.Health` jobs have existed since earlier this session's #307/#321 work but were **entirely dead**: `AutomationRunner.RunClaimed` enqueues a `"health-check"` `SyncRequest` for them and nothing anywhere in the codebase ever consumes that operation — confirmed by grep, not assumption.

`Catalog/XmlSourceHealthChecker.cs` (new): `XmlSourceHealthChecker.CheckAsync(HttpClient, XmlSource, CancellationToken, TimeSpan? timeout)` reuses the existing `ApiHealthClassifier.FromResponse`/`FromException` (`ApiHealth.cs`) for status classification instead of inventing a second scheme, adding only what that classifier doesn't cover: a `Stopwatch`-measured `LatencyMs`, and the local-file-vs-HTTPS distinction an XML source (unlike a marketplace API) actually has (an existing readable file is `HEALTHY` with near-zero latency and *no* network call at all; a missing/empty file is `CLIENT_ERROR`; a non-HTTPS, non-existent-file location is rejected before any request). A bounded linked-cancellation timeout (default 15s, matching the existing `EtsyHttp.SendJsonAsync`/`XmlSourceReader.ReadAsync` pattern) becomes `TIMEOUT`; genuine external cancellation propagates as `OperationCanceledException` rather than being misreported as a timeout — the same distinction those two existing methods already draw.

`Catalog/Models.cs`: `XmlSource` gained `LastHealthCheckUtc`, `LastHealthState` (default `"NEVER_CHECKED"`), `LastHealthHttpStatus`, `LastHealthLatencyMs`, `LastHealthError` — JSON-blob fields, zero migration, matching the Desi/PriceFields pattern from earlier this session.

`MainWindow.xaml.cs` `ScheduledAsync` (the real production entry point, not a new one): added a `dueHealth` set (every `Enabled` source whose `LastHealthCheckUtc` is stale beyond its own `IntervalMinutes`, independent of `AutoImport` — a source with auto-import off is still worth watching for reachability) and a loop that actually calls `XmlSourceHealthChecker.CheckAsync` and persists the result via the existing `store.SaveSource`. Skips a source that already ran a real import this same tick (that import attempt is itself a stronger reachability signal). Extended the #307 empty-tick guard (`due.Count==0&&dueJobs.Count==0`) to a three-way check so a health-only tick isn't skipped either.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/XmlSourceHealthCheckerTests.cs` (new, 8 tests): healthy response with measured latency, 404 classified as CLIENT_ERROR, 500 as SERVER_ERROR (distinct states), a slow feed past a 100ms bounded timeout classified TIMEOUT without hanging, genuine external cancellation propagating instead of being misreported as a timeout, an existing local file HEALTHY with *zero* network calls (proven via a handler that throws if ever invoked), a missing local file not silently HEALTHY, and an empty location short-circuiting to NOT_CONFIGURED. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SchedulerDecoupleTests.cs`: new `ScheduledTickPerformsAndPersistsAHealthCheckForADueEnabledSource` drives the real `ScheduledAsync` production path (not just the standalone checker) with a fake `HttpMessageHandler` — proving `ScheduledAsync` actually performs and persists the check, not merely that the checker function works in isolation. This is exactly the class of proof this session's #285/#276 work established is required: a standalone-helper test does not close a "wire it into the real path" gap.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=Catalog/XmlSourceHealthChecker.cs (new), Catalog/Models.cs, MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/XmlSourceHealthCheckerTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SchedulerDecoupleTests.cs; TEST_RESULT=193 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 184); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 9b805bc78a1c1db0efba08a73bfe1d288a992931749909f7c36ee00bb596989e.
Not done: no UI surface for the persisted health fields yet beyond what's already shown by the pre-existing local-summary `RefreshXmlSourceHealthAsync`/`xmlSourceHealth` TextBlock (a follow-up could show `LastHealthLatencyMs`/`LastHealthHttpStatus` there); no alerting/notification on a source going unhealthy (that's #694's "feed fingerprint ve değişim oranı alarmı" territory, a separate issue); Basic-Auth-protected feeds get an unauthenticated reachability ping (a 401 response is itself informative via the AUTH_ERROR classification, but this does not verify the *configured* credentials work).

## #693 — XML content-type and payload signature validation (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-692-xml-source-health

New issue, no prior audit history. The core risk it names -- a well-formed-enough HTML error/login page can parse "successfully" as generic XML syntax (matching tags) -- is real: writing the failing tests first showed `<!DOCTYPE html>...` was already rejected by the existing `DtdProcessing.Prohibit` setting (a DOCTYPE declaration trips XML's DTD prohibition), but only with a generic "invalid XML" message, and a bare `text/html` content-type with a body that never triggers a DOCTYPE/DTD path was not rejected at all before the parser even ran.

`XmlSourceReader.cs` `ReadAsync`: added an explicit `Content-Type` check rejecting `text/html`/`application/xhtml+xml` before any parsing is attempted (fails fast, before downloading/decoding the body). `ParseAsync`: added an explicit empty-body check (was previously only caught indirectly via a confusing generic XML-parse-error message) and a new `EnsureLooksLikeXml` payload-signature sniff (peeks the first 4KB, skips a BOM, and rejects `<!DOCTYPE html`/`<html` or anything not starting with `<` with a specific HTML-aware message) so a server that *mislabels* its content-type (claims `text/xml` but actually returns an HTML error page) is still caught by the payload itself, not just the header. The pre-existing 25MB size cap and the `XmlReader`'s own encoding auto-detection (which already fails closed on a declared-vs-actual encoding mismatch via `XmlException`) needed no changes — confirmed by test, not assumption.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/XmlSourcePayloadValidationTests.cs` (new, 6 tests, all via `XmlSourceReader` with a fake `HttpMessageHandler` -- no real network): valid XML accepted normally; an honest `text/html` content-type rejected before parsing even when the body would otherwise parse as valid XML syntax; a *mislabeled* `text/xml` response whose body is actually an HTML error page rejected by its own signature; an empty body rejected with a specific message; an oversized payload rejected without buffering it in full; a declared-UTF-16-but-actually-UTF-8 payload rejected instead of silently importing garbled text.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=XmlSourceReader.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/XmlSourcePayloadValidationTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=199 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 193); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 36d6ca72a4c5e6db4c94576ea108dc87823983e627554a5e8255f2ca773a9d2d.
Not done: the content-type check is a narrow allowlist-of-rejections (HTML family only) rather than a strict allowlist-of-acceptances, to avoid false-rejecting real supplier feeds served with a generic/incorrect-but-not-HTML content-type (e.g. `text/plain`, `application/octet-stream`), which are common in practice; a stricter policy was deliberately not adopted without evidence it wouldn't break real feeds.

## #694 — feed anomaly alarm re-verified against the real import path (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-692-xml-source-health

New issue; substantially overlaps #283's already-contested anomaly-detection territory (4 prior audit rounds, still open) but is narrower. Read the current code before writing anything: `Catalog/CatalogStore.cs`'s `Import` already calls `EnsureDropshipFeedSafe` -> `DropshipAnomalyGuard.Evaluate` on every import, and that guard already checks `PRODUCT_COUNT_SPIKE` (covers mass addition), `MISSING_PRODUCTS` (mass deletion), `MASS_ZERO_STOCK`, and `PRICE_JUMP` -- all *before* the write transaction starts (fail-closed). The one existing test exercising this via the real path (`SafetyAndStabilityTests.CatalogImport_BlocksMassZeroSupplierFeedBeforeWrite`) only covered the zero-stock case. #694's own TEST list (unchanged, normal delta, mass deletion, mass addition, restart) was **not** actually proven end-to-end anywhere; `DropshipAnomalyGuardFailsClosedAndAuditsOverride` (also pre-existing) only exercises the standalone `DropshipAnomalyGuard` class directly, the same "helper works in isolation, real path unproven" gap pattern this session's #285/#276/#692 work kept finding.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/FeedAnomalyRealImportPathTests.cs` (new, 5 tests, all via the real `CatalogStore.Import`, no production code changed -- the wiring was already correct): an identical re-submitted feed is not flagged; a 10% count delta is allowed (and clarifies, by assertion, that import is additive/upsert-only -- a normal non-complete batch never deletes products merely absent from it, so "allowed" means "not blocked," not "catalog shrank"); a 75% count drop (mass deletion) is blocked with the catalog left completely unchanged; a 200% count jump (mass addition, e.g. a corrupted/duplicated feed) is blocked the same way; and — the one scenario with no prior direct proof — a **second, independent `CatalogStore` instance pointed at the same directory** (simulating an app restart) still uses the persisted catalog as its anomaly baseline and correctly blocks a mass-deletion feed, rather than treating the restarted process as having no history.

**Not done, deliberately left to #283 rather than re-litigated here:** `EnsureDropshipFeedSafe` hardcodes `CategoryChanges`/`BrandChanges` to `0,0` when building the current-run metrics, so `MASS_TAXONOMY_CHANGE` can never actually fire — this is a real, narrow gap, but it's #283's own previously-identified territory (a 4-times-audited, still-open issue with much larger stated scope: persistent supplier profiles, override audit trail durable across restart, cross-supplier isolation). Fixing the taxonomy-change wiring in isolation here, under a different issue number, would risk exactly the kind of fragmented, duplicate-effort problem #283's own audit trail repeatedly warns against ("aynı düzeltmeyi iki kere yapma"). Content-fingerprint comparison (as opposed to count/price aggregates) was also not added, for the same reason: #694's own use of the word "fingerprint" most likely refers to this count/price-aggregate comparison already in place, not a content-hash diff, and inventing a new content-hash-based anomaly dimension without a concrete requirement for it would be scope invention, not a fix.

## #784/#785 — safe exception serialization + local path disclosure guard (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-784-785-safe-exception-serialization off codex/issue-692-xml-source-health

New issues, no prior audit history. Found the concrete gap by reading the existing code: `AuditStore.Sanitize` (the codebase's central log/audit redaction function, already covering Authorization headers, query-string secrets, key=value secrets, and email/phone PII) had **no rule at all for a local file path** -- `C:\Users\<real-username>\...` would pass through untouched into any log line, support export, or audit row. Separately, grep confirmed **no global exception handler existed anywhere** (`Application.DispatcherUnhandledException`, `AppDomain.UnhandledException`, `TaskScheduler.UnobservedTaskException` all unwired) -- an exception that reached the top of the stack today would hit .NET's default unhandled-exception behavior with zero safe diagnostic captured first, and every existing call site that DOES catch exceptions (`sync.Fail(id, error.Message)`, `MainWindow.Log`, etc.) only ever kept `.Message`, discarding the stack trace and inner-exception chain entirely.

`AuditStore.cs` `Sanitize`: added two regexes redacting only the username segment of a Windows (`C:\Users\<name>\...`) or Unix/macOS (`/home/<name>/...`, `/Users/<name>/...`) profile path, leaving the rest of the path intact (still useful for diagnostics: "yes, this was under AppData\Local\Temp") and leaving a path with no such segment (relative paths, `Program Files`, etc.) completely untouched -- confirmed by a dedicated negative test.

`SafeExceptionSerializer.cs` (new): `Serialize(Exception, maxDepth=5)` walks the full inner-exception chain (bounded depth, since an unusually deep chain is a realistic risk even though .NET's own chain cannot literally cycle), sanitizing each exception's `Message` and `StackTrace` **independently** rather than sanitizing one giant joined string -- avoiding `Sanitize`'s existing 2000-char cap ever truncating a legitimately long stack trace.

`App.xaml.cs`: was a fully empty template with zero exception handling. Added `OnStartup` wiring for all three global handlers, each calling a new `LogCrash` that writes the sanitized report (with a UTC timestamp) to `%LocalAppData%\MonoBridgeDesktop\crash.log`, wrapped in its own try/catch so a failure while logging a crash can never mask the original exception. Deliberately does **not** set `DispatcherUnhandledException.Handled = true`: a genuinely unhandled exception means the app's state is no longer trustworthy, and this handler's job is to leave a safe diagnostic before the normal crash proceeds, not to keep a corrupted app alive.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SafeExceptionSerializationTests.cs` (new, 9 tests): a Windows user-profile path has its username redacted; the same holds for a path under that profile's Temp folder; a normal relative path is left byte-for-byte untouched (proves no over-redaction); `Serialize` preserves exception type, message, and a real stack trace (not a placeholder); it walks a genuine inner-exception chain; a secret embedded in a message is redacted; a local path embedded in a message is redacted; a bearer token embedded in an HTTP error message is redacted (via the pre-existing `Sanitize` pattern, confirming the wiring reuses it rather than reimplementing); and an unusually deep exception chain is capped rather than walked without bound. `App.LogCrash`'s file-write side effect was deliberately left untested directly (it targets the same fixed real `%LocalAppData%` path as `CredentialStore` did before this session's earlier fix) -- its only real logic is `SafeExceptionSerializer.Serialize`, already fully covered.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=AuditStore.cs, SafeExceptionSerializer.cs (new), App.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SafeExceptionSerializationTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=213 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 204); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 747b71e0f7e4f1dcb231e011588cc7d6882abbd7987456c8126b6b8e6eba640f.
Not done: existing catch sites throughout the app (`sync.Fail(id, error.Message)`, `MainWindow.Log`, job-failure records) still only persist `.Message`, not the full `SafeExceptionSerializer.Serialize` report -- retrofitting every one of those call sites to capture full stack/inner-exception detail is a much larger, higher-blast-radius change than adding the previously-nonexistent global safety net, and was left out of this pass; "token-like value" detection is limited to the pre-existing keyword/header-based patterns in `Sanitize` (e.g. `Authorization: Bearer ...`, `key=value`) -- a bare high-entropy string with no such marker is not detected, since heuristic secret-shape detection risks false-positive redaction of legitimate non-secret values (SKUs, GUIDs) without a concrete pattern to anchor on.

## #786 — clipboard copy redaction core (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-784-785-safe-exception-serialization

New issue. Checked the premise before writing anything: grep found **zero** `Clipboard.*` call sites anywhere in this app -- there is no copy-to-clipboard action on any diagnostic, order, or customer screen today. The issue's own wording ("copy-to-clipboard aksiyonlarında... classification uygula") assumes such actions already exist and just need classification added; they don't. Building actual copy commands across the diagnostics/order/customer screens would be new UI feature work on multiple screens, not a security fix to existing functionality, so that was deliberately not invented here.

`ClipboardRedaction.cs` (new): the classification/masking core a future copy command can call. Reuses `AuditStore.Sanitize` for content-based detection (if sanitizing a value changes it, the value contained something Sanitize already knows to redact — a secret, a token, PII, or a local path) rather than duplicating pattern-matching; adds a small explicit field-name list (`Token`, `Password`, `Secret`, etc.) for fields whose *entire* value is inherently sensitive, where content-based detection has nothing to anchor on and full masking (not partial, in-place redaction) is the safer choice.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ClipboardRedactionTests.cs` (new, 5 tests): a field named as a secret is never copied raw regardless of its value; a bearer token embedded in a generic field's text is redacted by content; an email or phone number embedded in generic text is redacted; plain non-sensitive text is copied byte-for-byte unchanged (proves no over-redaction); and a mixed multi-field "selection" is judged field-by-field rather than masking the whole batch because one field was sensitive, or letting one sensitive field slip through because most weren't.
