Not done: no actual copy-to-clipboard UI commands exist yet on any screen (diagnostics, order, customer, or elsewhere) for this to be wired into -- that is a separate, larger feature-build task, not a redaction fix, and is explicitly out of scope for this pass.

## #779 — SQLite busy/locked diagnostics (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-779-sqlite-busy-diagnostics off codex/issue-784-785-safe-exception-serialization

New issue. Found the existing state by reading the code first: `SqliteConnectionPolicy.Open` (the shared connection factory most stores already use) already sets `PRAGMA busy_timeout=15000` on every connection -- SQLite's own engine already waits/retries internally for 15 seconds before ever raising SQLITE_BUSY/SQLITE_LOCKED. That is already a real, working retry mechanism; the gap was what happens once that window genuinely expires. `MainWindow.Safe(Exception)` (the central point most operations funnel their error message through for the user) classified anything that wasn't `InvalidOperationException`/`ArgumentException` into one generic "İşlem tamamlanamadı. Dosya biçimini, erişim izinlerini ve bağlantıyı kontrol et." bucket -- a persistent database lock would land there, indistinguishable from a corrupt file or a permissions problem, with no signal to the user that simply retrying in a few seconds might work.

`SqliteBusyDiagnostics.cs` (new): `IsBusyOrLocked(Exception)` recognizes a real `SqliteException` with `SqliteErrorCode` 5 (SQLITE_BUSY) or 6 (SQLITE_LOCKED); `Describe` returns a clear, actionable Turkish message for that case. `MainWindow.xaml.cs` `Safe(Exception e)`: checks this classification first, before falling through to the existing generic buckets.

Deliberately did **not** add a second, application-level retry loop on top of SQLite's own 15-second `busy_timeout` wait: a "transient lock" already resolves transparently within that window with no code change needed, and a "persistent lock" should fail with a clear message promptly once the window expires, not be made to wait even longer by an additional retry layer stacked on top.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SqliteBusyDiagnosticsTests.cs` (new, 3 tests, all against a **real** SQLite lock, not a hand-constructed fake exception): one connection holds an uncommitted write transaction on a file while a second, contending connection attempts a write and receives a genuine `SqliteException`, correctly classified and described; an unrelated `SqliteException` (querying a nonexistent table) is not misclassified as busy; a non-SQLite exception is never misclassified. Along the way, discovered and worked around a real Microsoft.Data.Sqlite gotcha worth recording: its `SqliteConnectionStringBuilder.DefaultTimeout` connection-string property silently overrides a `PRAGMA busy_timeout` issued after `Open()`, and `DefaultTimeout=0` means "wait forever" (following ADO.NET `CommandTimeout` convention), not "fail immediately" -- the first version of this test hung for that exact reason before being fixed to use `DefaultTimeout=1`.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=SqliteBusyDiagnostics.cs (new), MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/SqliteBusyDiagnosticsTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=221 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 218); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 9c4e5195183b27195bd570658ce4b72bbf2e0722476aacd3647eadc61518e83e.
Not done: `Safe(Exception)` is the central point for most MainWindow-originated operations, but not the only catch site in the app (e.g., `AutomationRunner.RunClaimed`'s per-product catch already records `error.Message` directly into a `SyncRequest` failure without going through `Safe`) -- a full sweep of every catch site to route through this classifier was not attempted, since most of those are backend job-failure records rather than user-facing terminal diagnostics, which is what the issue's own "ENTRY: DB write/read critical paths" points at.

## #780 — WAL checkpoint health diagnostics (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-779-sqlite-busy-diagnostics

New issue. `DatabaseHealth.Inspect` (the existing "ENTRY: health/maintenance checks" point) already covered `quick_check`, schema version, `journal_mode`, and `foreign_keys` via a read-only connection; it reported nothing about the WAL file's actual size or whether a checkpoint could make progress.

`DatabaseHealth.cs`: `DatabaseHealthResult` gained two additive fields (`WalSizeBytes`, `CheckpointBusy`, both defaulted so every existing call site is unaffected). A new `InspectWal` reads the `.db-wal` file's real size directly from the filesystem (zero risk, no connection needed) and separately opens a short-lived, properly read-write connection (the existing checks stay on the read-only one) to run `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(PASSIVE)` -- SQLite's own non-disruptive checkpoint mode, which never blocks or forces out a concurrent reader/writer, so this is safe to run against a live, in-use database as a routine health check. No size or duration threshold is hardcoded anywhere, per the issue's own "hardcoded tuning yok" -- the raw byte count is reported as-is.

Writing the "locked checkpoint" test first caught a real distinction worth recording: `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint`'s `busy` column alone only means the checkpoint *couldn't even start* (e.g. a conflicting checkpoint already in progress); a long-open reader instead lets it run to completion but only partially (`checkpointed < log` in the pragma's own result columns) without ever setting `busy`. The initial implementation only looked at `busy` and the "locked checkpoint" test failed for exactly this reason (a single open reader transaction correctly did *not* set busy=1) -- fixed by treating `busy || checkpointed < log` as the single "needs attention" signal, since both are legitimately the same "the WAL isn't shrinking" condition from a diagnostic's point of view.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/WalCheckpointHealthTests.cs` (new, 4 tests, all against a real SQLite WAL file, not mocked): a fresh WAL with no contention checkpoints cleanly; a WAL grown by 2000 uncheckpointed inserts (while a held reader prevents checkpointing) reports its actual multi-hundred-KB size, not a guess; an open reader blocking a full checkpoint is reported as busy without throwing or hanging; and the WAL size is still correctly read after closing and reopening all connections (simulating a restart).

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=DatabaseHealth.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/WalCheckpointHealthTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=225 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 221); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 72929e15cf666ddc35c8adf161e1d33148f6ed4de4f540af0ef8c990acec458e.
Not done: no UI surface displays these new fields yet (the existing health check has no visible panel in the app that this session found wired to it either, beyond whatever calls `DatabaseHealth.Inspect` directly); no automatic corrective action (e.g. triggering a more aggressive checkpoint mode) is taken when a checkpoint is reported as blocked -- this issue asked for diagnostics, not remediation, and taking an aggressive checkpoint action automatically would risk exactly the kind of disruptive behavior PASSIVE mode was chosen to avoid.

## #781 — order list query plan (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-781-order-list-query-plan off codex/issue-779-sqlite-busy-diagnostics

New issue. Measured the real query plan before changing anything: `OrdersStore.ReadPage` filters by `marketplace`+`shop` (the common "one store's order list" view) and always sorts by `json_extract(payload,'$.UpdatedAt') DESC`, but the three existing indexes were each single-purpose (marketplace+shop alone; status alone; UpdatedAt alone) -- none of them let SQLite satisfy both the filter and the sort from one index walk. `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` on the real query confirmed exactly this: `SEARCH orders USING INDEX IX_orders_marketplace_shop (...) | USE TEMP B-TREE FOR ORDER BY` -- a real, measurable extra sort step on every single call to the most common order-list view, not a micro-optimization.

`OrdersStore.cs`: added `IX_orders_shop_updated ON orders(marketplace,shop,json_extract(payload,'$.UpdatedAt') DESC)` -- a compound index covering both the filter and the sort together, so the query can be satisfied by one index walk. `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` on an already-open database is a no-op cost-wise for existing installs (SQLite skips it if present) and adds the index on next open otherwise -- no migration step needed.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/OrderListQueryPlanTests.cs` (new, 2 tests): `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` on the store-scoped, date-sorted query no longer contains `TEMP B-TREE` and does show `USING INDEX` (deterministic, machine-speed-independent proof, not a timing assertion); and a realistic 5,000-order dataset across two marketplaces and two shops still returns the correct page (right count, correctly filtered, correctly ordered newest-first) after the index change.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=OrdersStore.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/OrderListQueryPlanTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=227 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 225); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 37b300bee9f350adb76de3ebd13f184cb4d2db76de4aa673293dc2d3eb5b9d50.
Not done: the issue's own "100k orders" test scale was not run as a wall-clock timing benchmark (5,000 rows was used for the functional-correctness test); the `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` structural proof is the primary evidence here since it is dataset-size-independent -- the query plan a compound index produces at 5,000 rows is the same plan it produces at 100,000, so a much larger synthetic dataset would mostly cost test time without adding proof; the status+date-sort combination (a query filtering by `RawStatus` and sorting by `UpdatedAt`) was not given its own compound index, since `ReadPage`'s status filter is typically combined with a marketplace/shop scope already covered by the new index, and adding an index for every possible filter combination without a demonstrated real query shape hitting it would be speculative tuning.

## #777 — orphan reference audit in the health check (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-777-orphan-reference-audit off codex/issue-781-order-list-query-plan

New issue. Checked what "foreign key" even means here before writing anything: this app's product/source/mapping relationships are *logical* references -- `CatalogProducts.$.SourceId` inside a JSON blob pointing at `Sources.Id`, and `MarketplaceMappings.LocalId` pointing at `CatalogProducts.Id` -- not declared SQL foreign keys. So `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` can never see them, and `PRAGMA foreign_keys` being on or off is irrelevant to them; an audit has to resolve them itself. No `DeleteSource` exists in the store today, so an orphan product is a corruption/migration artifact rather than a normal-flow outcome, which is exactly what a diagnostic audit is for.

`DatabaseHealth.cs` (the existing "ENTRY: health check"): `DatabaseHealthResult` gained three additive, defaulted fields (`OrphanProductCount`, `OrphanMappingCount`, `RepairPreview`) so every existing caller and the pre-existing `DatabaseHealthInspectsReadOnlySqliteAndDetectsMissingDatabase` test are unaffected. New `InspectReferences` runs on the same read-only connection as the integrity checks and is a repair *preview* (counts plus up to three sample ids per category, surfaced in `Detail` as "Sahipsiz kayıt: ..."), never a repair. Two deliberate scoping choices: an empty `SourceId` is "no source" by design and is not counted as dangling; and `MarketplaceMappings` is created lazily by `MarketplaceMappingStore`, so a database that predates it simply has no such table -- treated as a legitimate legacy state yielding zero orphan mappings, not an error.

That legacy-robustness point bit immediately in the other direction: the first version assumed `CatalogProducts`/`Sources` always exist, and the four `WalCheckpointHealthTests` (#780) -- which build a bare `catalog.db` with raw SQL and no catalog schema -- flipped the whole health check to `ERROR` via "no such table". Fixed by guarding those two tables the same way: a partially-initialized or foreign `catalog.db` still gets the integrity check, with nothing to audit. The suite caught a real gap in the new code, not a test-side artifact.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/OrphanReferenceAuditTests.cs` (new, 4 tests matching the issue's own TEST list, all against a real `catalog.db` built by the real stores): a valid database reports 0/0 and an empty preview; two products whose `Sources` row was deleted underneath them plus one mapping pointing at a nonexistent product are counted exactly (and the valid mapping is not), with both ids named in the preview; a legacy database with no `MarketplaceMappings` table and `foreign_keys` at SQLite's default (off) is still fully audited without turning into an error; and the preview is read-only and idempotent (row count unchanged after two inspections, identical output both times).

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=DatabaseHealth.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/OrphanReferenceAuditTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=231 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 227); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 8f5877e08efb16842b56c6a9382b1b86cd87b9f1e329e69ac7467998163e42cc.
Not done: no actual repair action (deleting orphan mappings, or re-parenting/quarantining orphan products) -- the issue asked for an audit with a repair *preview*, and an automatic delete of rows on a health check would be exactly the kind of unrequested destructive action a diagnostic must not take; cross-database references (`media.db`'s `ProductMedia` -> product id, `SyncJobs.EntityId`, which doubles as an Etsy listing id for the etsy channel and so cannot be resolved as a product id) were not audited; orphans do not change `Status` (still integrity/schema-driven) -- they are reported in their own fields and in `Detail`.

## #778 — transaction boundary audit of multi-step mutations (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-778-transaction-boundaries off codex/issue-777-orphan-reference-audit

New issue. This one is an audit first, so the deliverable had to start with an honest inventory rather than a speculative refactor. Read every multi-step product/order/import mutation: `CatalogStore.ImportCore` (products plus the source-state row in one non-deferred transaction, `ReconcileMissingSource` deliberately after commit because it writes a different database), `SaveProduct`/`DeleteProduct`/`Undo`/`SaveSource` (each one transaction), `OrdersStore.Save` (one transaction per batch, upsert per order), `MarketplaceMappingStore.Save` (single upsert), and every lease/claim in `XmlRunStore`, `AutomationStore` and `SyncStore` (`TryStart`, `TryClaimLease`, `Complete`/`Fail` are single conditional `UPDATE`s -- atomic by construction; `AutomationStore.Complete/Fail` do a read first, but the read is not a write, and the `WHERE LeaseToken=$token` guard is what makes them safe). Result: those boundaries are already correct.

The one real half-commit was in `AutomationRunner.RunClaimed`'s per-product catch block: `sync.Enqueue(...)` (an `INSERT` in `Pending`) followed by `sync.Fail(id, message)` (an `UPDATE` to `Failed`) -- two statements on two connections with nothing tying them together. Between them the `":error"`-payload job is `Pending`, i.e. exactly the state `TryStart` dispatches; a crash there (or the `Fail` call throwing, e.g. `SQLITE_BUSY`) leaves a bogus job live in the queue whose payload is the literal word `error`. Fixed with `SyncStore.EnqueueFailed(request, error)`: one `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT(Channel,ShopId,Operation,EntityId,Version) DO UPDATE` written directly in `Failed` with `FailureCount=1`, so no `Pending` state ever exists for a failure record, and a repeat of the same failure bumps the count instead of creating a duplicate -- the same idempotency contract `Enqueue` already has. `RunClaimed` now calls it; nothing else changed.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/TransactionBoundaryTests.cs` (new, 4 tests, real stores). The interesting one proves the window rather than just the end state: a `SyncJobsTrace` table fed by `AFTER INSERT`/`AFTER UPDATE` triggers records every status the row ever had, at machine speed and independent of timing. Run against the old two-call code (fix stashed) it fails with `Expected:<0>. Actual:<1>` -- the row was observably `Pending` -- and passes with the atomic write; the failure is injected through the real path (a price policy with no expense fields is blocked by the #285 money preflight inside `PreviewPrice`). The other three: same failure twice → one row, `FailureCount=2`, never `Pending`; an import batch whose third row is a duplicate SKU rolls back rows one and two *and* the source-state row to the last committed feed (`feed-1`, count 3 -- verified against a real prior commit, not an empty database); and a failure record can still be retried by an operator while a job orphaned `Running` by a crash is recovered to `Pending` on the next launch without being duplicated (retry + restart scenarios from the issue's TEST list).

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=Catalog/SyncStore.cs, Catalog/AutomationRunner.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/TransactionBoundaryTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=235 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 231); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 4c1e8aadbd9138be2d14c39649e97b5ef95446e01e6167801dcc2b3e6c703ad4.
Not done: `SyncStore.Enqueue` is find-then-`INSERT` (two statements) -- not a half-commit, but two concurrent enqueues of the same key would make the loser throw `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT` instead of returning the existing row; left as is because there is one scheduler thread today, noted for when there is not. `ImportCore` + `ReconcileMissingSource` span two databases (`catalog.db`, `source-health.db`) and cannot share a transaction; a crash between them loses one quarantine observation, which the next complete feed re-derives, so no data is permanently wrong -- documented, not changed. No cross-database two-phase commit was attempted.

## #783 — dashboard aggregate snapshot cache (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-783-dashboard-aggregate-cache off codex/issue-778-transaction-boundaries

New issue. ENTRY is `DashboardPanel` -> `DashboardDataService.Load()`, which is the first thing shown after every navigation and aggregates every store from scratch each time: all products, all orders, all sync jobs, the last 100 XML runs, sources, connections and the data-quality summary. `DashboardPanel.Create` also constructs a fresh service per navigation, so any cache had to be process-wide, keyed by data directory, to ever be warm on the real path.

`DashboardData.cs`: `Load()` is now `Load(bypassCache)` over a `ConcurrentDictionary<directory, (revision, storedUtc, snapshot)>`, with the old body as `Build()`. An entry is served only when it is younger than `MaxAge` (default 10 s, injectable with a clock for tests) *and* its revision still matches. "Revision-aware" is done without touching a single store: the revision is computed from the SQLite files themselves -- name, length and the first 32 bytes (WAL header salts / checkpoint sequence, or the rollback-journal change counter) of every `*.db` and `*.db-wal` under the directory, read through a file handle so an open writer's bytes are seen rather than stale directory metadata (`-shm` is excluded because readers touch it). Every committed write appends WAL frames or rewrites the WAL header, so a product import, a source save, an order sync or a job state change from this or any other process invalidates the entry immediately; the TTL is only the bound on staleness if something ever escaped that signal. Two details that matter: the revision is captured *after* the build, because opening the stores itself writes (schema checks, and `SyncStore.Open` rebuilds its unique index on every open) and those writes must not read as "changed" on the very next load; and a failed build throws before anything is stored, so a failure is never cached and never masks the next attempt. `DashboardPanel.cs`: navigation loads through the cache; the user's explicit "Durumu yenile" click calls `Load(bypassCache: true)` -- an explicit refresh is a request for an authoritative re-read and must never be answered from a cache.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DashboardAggregateCacheTests.cs` (new, 6 tests, real stores in temp directories). Cache behaviour is judged by identity (`ReferenceEquals` on the immutable snapshot record), which is deterministic and machine-speed independent, unlike timing. Written red first: the warm-load test fails against the old service because every `Load()` produced a new instance. Covered: warm load across two service instances returns the same snapshot; a product import (a second supplier's first feed -- the first version of this test tripped the #776 dropship anomaly gate with a +100% count spike on the same source, which was the test's mistake, not the cache's) and then a `SaveSource` each invalidate immediately with the right counts; a store switch serves each directory its own snapshot and switching back is still warm; past `MaxAge` the snapshot is rebuilt with no disk change; an explicit refresh bypasses and replaces the entry; and a store corrupted underneath a warm cache makes the next load throw `SqliteException` rather than serve the stale snapshot, after which a repaired store loads from scratch (the failure was not cached).

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=DashboardData.cs, DashboardPanel.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DashboardAggregateCacheTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=241 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 235); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 5b338b9fa8ad2cc83327ae6a9a618d962f02e849b5e3fa2ff23e60c732549f55.
Not done: no per-query caching inside `Build()` (each aggregate still reads its whole table when a rebuild happens; the issue asked for the aggregate *result* to be cached, which this is); the dashboard's 14-day order trend still uses `DateTime.Today`, so a cached snapshot can be up to `MaxAge` stale across midnight; `SyncStore.Open` dropping and recreating `UX_SyncJobs_Key` on every open is a real write-per-read cost discovered here and left for its own issue rather than folded into this one; no UI-level test of the "Durumu yenile" bypass (the wiring is a one-line argument change, exercised through the service tests).

## #782 — order timeline lazy loading (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-782-order-timeline-lazy-loading off codex/issue-783-dashboard-aggregate-cache

New issue. ENTRY is the order detail pane: `OrdersPanel.Edit` -> `ShipmentEditor`, which created one `TextBlock` per observation for the whole shipment history in a single loop on the UI thread, every time an order or shipment was selected. The events are already in memory (they travel inside the order's JSON payload), so the cost is element creation and layout, and for a long history that is a UI-thread stall on every selection.

`OrderTimelineLoader.cs` (new, no WPF dependency): orders newest-first (as before), owns the cursor, and appends one page (50) at a time in chunks (25) through a caller-supplied sink, awaiting a caller-supplied `yield` between chunks. Two properties carry the issue's guarantees: the cursor advances per item actually appended, so a cancelled page leaves the cursor exactly at what is on screen and a later resume neither skips nor repeats; and a request while a page is in flight, or for a cursor that is not the current position, appends nothing -- a double click or a late scroll event can never append the same page twice. `ShouldLoadOnScroll` is the scroll predicate (bottom within 64 px, or content shorter than the viewport; an unmeasured pane never triggers). `OrdersPanel.cs`: `ShipmentEditor` renders the header count, a timeline panel, and a "Daha fazla gözlem yükle (n kaldı)" button; the yield is `Dispatcher.Yield(DispatcherPriority.Background)` so input is processed between chunks; the detail `ScrollViewer`'s `ScrollChanged` requests the next page near the bottom; `Edit` (refresh / order switch), shipment switch and `Unloaded` cancel the in-flight page through a per-editor `CancellationTokenSource`.

Tests, red first (the loader tests failed to compile without the class). `OrderTimelineLazyLoadingTests.cs` (6): 1000 events paged newest-first across 20 pages with no loss or duplication and exactly one yield per page; duplicate request while a page is in flight, a stale cursor and an arbitrary cursor all append nothing while the correct cursor continues; refresh restarts from the newest event with the identical first page; cancellation stops at a chunk boundary, the cursor counts only what was appended, and a fresh token resumes without gaps; the scroll predicate; an empty history. `OrderTimelineUiTests.cs` (1, on the real entry path): a real `OrdersStore` with a 1000-observation order and a 3-observation order, the real `OrdersPanel`, selection through the real `DataGrid` -- opening renders exactly 50 lines, one click appends 50 more, two rapid clicks append exactly one page (the second is rejected as a duplicate of the page in flight), and switching orders while a page is in flight shows only the new order's 3 lines with the "more" button collapsed.

That UI test found a real subtlety worth recording rather than a bug in the feature: on a bare STA test thread with no `SynchronizationContext`, the loader's `await yield()` continuation was resumed on the thread pool -- `Dispatcher.Yield` completes *inside* a dispatcher operation, where WPF installs a `DispatcherSynchronizationContext`, so the runtime refuses to inline a continuation that was registered without one. The production UI thread always has that context (continuations post back at Normal priority), and the existing MainWindow tests install it by hand; the new test now does the same. Diagnosed with a throwaway experiment (three pumping strategies, the wrapped `Func<Task>`, and the real loader), deleted afterwards. Also learned in the same test: `OrdersStore.SaveManual` appends its own state observation when the shipment state differs from the last event's, which is why the seeded 1000 becomes 1001 and the seeded 3 stays 3 -- asserted as such.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=OrderTimelineLoader.cs (new), OrdersPanel.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/OrderTimelineLazyLoadingTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/OrderTimelineUiTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=248 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 241); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 a200f014d61e006e8af5f0b9568011bdea4df63cc61965d58edee7dc3bdc6fdd.
Not done: the scroll-driven paging is covered only by the predicate test, not by a laid-out UI test (the detail pane is never measured in the UI test, deliberately, so paging there is button-driven and deterministic); the rest of the order detail (items, stock preview) is still rendered eagerly -- those lists are small by nature; no UI virtualization (`ListBox`/`VirtualizingStackPanel`) was introduced, because the issue asked for lazy/paged loading and virtualization would have changed the pane's selection and keyboard behaviour; the per-event `TextBlock` is still a wrapped text block rather than a lighter run.

## #790 — pricing provenance integration suite (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-790-pricing-provenance-suite off codex/issue-782-order-timeline-lazy-loading

New issue (TEST). The suite runs the whole chain -- FX, commission, shipping, VAT, rounding, minimum-margin guard -- through the real pricing entry point `CatalogStore.PreviewPrice` (and `AutomationRunner.RunDue` where dispatch is the point) against a real `catalog.db`, never the standalone calculator. Fixture: cost 100 TRY, commission 20% of sale, shipping 10 TRY, so a TRY sale S nets 0.8·S − 110 and the zero-contribution point is S = 137.50 exactly; every expectation below is derived from that by hand.

Writing the suite found a real regression, which is the whole reason a suite like this exists: `PricePolicy.MinimumMarginTry` -- the operator's "at least N TRY per sale" -- is edited in `MainWindow.PricePolicies`, shown in `PolicyCenterPanel`, copied by `PolicyCenterStore`, validated non-negative on save, and **enforced nowhere**. Before #285 it was compared with the naive formula-minus-cost (which ignored every fee); #285 replaced that check with the money gate and dropped the guard entirely, so a policy with a 25 TRY minimum happily queued a price whose real contribution was 18 TRY (naive diff +60). Fixed in `PreviewPrice` right after the money preflight: `money.NetContribution < p.MinimumMarginTry` throws `"Fiyat gönderimi engellendi: net katkı 18.00 TRY, asgari kâr 25.00 TRY altında; local/min-25."` -- the guard now means what the operator typed, after commission, shipping, transaction cost and VAT, and the message carries the numbers and the channel/shop it applies to. Invariant formatting so the text is culture-independent.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/PricingProvenanceSuiteTests.cs` (new, 6 tests; the two minimum-margin tests were red first -- one on `PreviewPrice`, one on the real automation dispatch which queued the under-margin price). Rounding boundary: `x*1.375` (137.500, net exactly 0) and `x*1.375005` (→137.50) are blocked, `x*1.37505` rounds half-away-from-zero to 137.51 and passes with the preview showing 137.51 -- banker's rounding would have blocked it. Tax change: 160 TRY is Ready at 0% VAT, becomes `BlockedNegativeMargin` once the operator records 20% VAT *included* (133.33 − 32 − 10 − 100 = −8.67), and stays Ready with the same 20% on a VAT-exclusive price. Missing fee: each of shipping/commission/VAT/transaction missing on its own blocks with `BlockedMissingInput`; the complete policy is the positive control. FX leg: a USD policy at 30 TRY/USD with a 1-hour-old observation converts 159.90 TRY to 5.33 USD and the preview carries the provenance (`FormulaPriceTry` 159.9, `CostTry` 100, currency USD); a 25-hour-old observation is `BlockedStaleFx`; no observation at all is `BlockedMissingInput`. Minimum margin: 25 blocks an 18.00 contribution, exactly 18 is allowed, 18.01 blocks; through `RunDue` nothing is queued and the recorded failure names the guard.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=Catalog/PricePolicy.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/PricingProvenanceSuiteTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=254 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 248); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 2fbfad4bdc3528ffb26391b1b3f69008857d4b714f9647a7dda8ee29006cbc50.
Not done: `PricePreview` still does not carry the net contribution / status itself (the guard's numbers live in the block message; adding `NetContributionTry` to the preview record would be the next provenance step and touches the UI preview grid); `MinimumPrice` (the floor on the sale price) was already enforced and is not re-tested here; the `PriceFieldName` path shares the same gate and guard but the suite exercises only the formula path; no test of `PolicyCenterStore`'s policy copy carrying `MinimumMarginTry` across shops.

## #787 — store isolation integration suite (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-787-store-isolation-suite off codex/issue-790-pricing-provenance-suite

New issue (TEST). First question was what "store" means here, because the suite is only worth anything against the real model: there is one data directory per installation and no store switch in the app; the operator's stores are the marketplace shops (`ShopId`), and every shop-owned record is keyed by (channel|marketplace, shop, ...) -- orders, stock receipts/movements/restores, price and stock policies, listing mappings, sync jobs, channel plans, connections. The product master and the XML supplier sources are deliberately shared (one catalog, many storefronts). So isolation means: shop A's records are never visible to, borrowed by, or overwritten from shop B's scope, through the real repository/service entry points (`OrdersStore.ReadPage`, `OrderStockDecisionService`, `CatalogStore.PreviewPrice` + `AutomationRunner.RunDue`, `MarketplaceMappingStore`, `OrderTransferCodec`, `GlobalSearchIndexService`), never raw SQL; and the unscoped reads that do exist (`ReadAll`, global search) must attribute every record to its shop rather than merge identities.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/StoreIsolationSuiteTests.cs` (new, 6 tests, all with two shops holding an order under the *same* number `o-1`): shop-scoped order read/search/report -- shop A's `o-1` is shop A's record, a text search issued in shop B's scope never finds shop A's item, an unknown shop sees nothing, a product-order report fed from shop B's scoped read counts nothing of shop A's, and the unscoped read is an explicit all-shops view; a stock deduction applied for shop A's `o-1` neither marks shop B's `o-1` as already applied nor blocks it, while both draw once from the one shared stock; a price policy is never borrowed across shops and the two shops' automation jobs produce one sync record each, shop A's staying `Pending` while shop B's fails for lack of a policy; listing mappings resolve only within their shop and both shops may map the same product to the same external id without overwriting each other; an order export built from a shop-scoped read carries only that shop and round-trips; global search returns two attributed hits for `o-1`, never one merged record.

Red first, and the red was a real defect rather than a missing isolation: the shop-scoped search `ReadPage(shopId: "shop-a", query: "Kırmızı")` returned 0 for an order whose item is literally "Kırmızı kupa". `OrdersStore.ReadPage` matches the query with `payload LIKE '%…%'` against the raw JSON payload, and `System.Text.Json` escapes every non-ASCII character on write (the dotless i is stored as a backslash-u escape), so any order search containing ı/ğ/ş/ç/ö/ü silently found nothing -- for a Turkish-language operator that is most product names. Fixed in `OrdersStore.cs` by matching the query in both forms: raw (ids, and any payload written unescaped) and JSON-encoded exactly as the serializer writes it (`JavaScriptEncoder.Default.Encode`); no migration, no change to what is stored, and the #781 compound-index query plan is unaffected (`OrderListQueryPlanTests` still pass).

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=OrdersStore.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/StoreIsolationSuiteTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=260 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 254); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 39ba1aadc0e46ee0ace5ef81773f3cd684627633969a057440467514d6b58de9.
Not done: the search fix makes Turkish text findable but is still case-sensitive for non-ASCII letters ("KIRMIZI" does not find "Kırmızı"; SQLite's LIKE folds ASCII only) -- a proper fix is a normalized search column, which is a schema change and its own issue; `ReturnManagement.ValidateBatch`'s shop filter was already covered by an existing test and is not repeated; `ChannelProductsStore`, `MarketplaceConnectionStore` and stock policies are keyed the same way but were not given their own cases; `PolicyCenterStore`'s explicit cross-shop policy copy is an operator action (allowed by design) and was noticed to drop the #285 expense fields on the copy -- fail-closed on the target shop, recorded here as a follow-up, not changed.

## #788 — return and refund reconciliation (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-788-return-refund-reconciliation off codex/issue-787-store-isolation-suite

New issue (TEST, but the inventory came first): returns and refunds had no reconciliation to test. What existed was `ReturnManagement` (shop scope + fingerprint dedupe of return cases, no quantities against anything), `EtsyOrderCompletion` (event dedupe, a refund *stock* preview with no amount), and the whole-order restore in `OrderStockRestore`, which puts back the full receipt quantity however much actually came back -- a partial return of 1 of 3 would have restored 3. Nothing compared a refund amount with the order total or a currency with the order's. So the suite needed a service under it, on the real path.

`Catalog/OrderReturnReconciliation.cs` (new, `CatalogStore` partial, same shape as the existing stock decisions): `PreviewOrderReturn(order, event)` / `ApplyOrderReturn(order, event, approved)` take the `OrderSnapshot` (fetched by the caller through the new exact lookup `OrdersStore.Find(marketplace, shop, orderId)`) and an `OrderReturnEvent` (event key, SKU, quantity, refund amount, currency), and reconcile it against five things: the ordered quantity of the SKU; the *fulfilled* quantity -- what the stock receipt deducted, i.e. what can physically come back (no receipt: the quantity still reconciles against the order but nothing is restored); the order total for cumulative refunds; the order currency (case-insensitive, and only when an amount is refunded); and the new ledger `OrderReturnEvents` (created lazily in `catalog.db`) of events already applied, keyed by event, so the same event is never counted or restored twice. Statuses: `OK_PARTIAL`, `OK_FULL`, `BLOCKED` (reasons `OVER_RETURN`, `OVER_REFUND`, `CURRENCY_MISMATCH`, `SKU_NOT_IN_ORDER`, `ORDER_SCOPE_MISMATCH`, `QUANTITY_INVALID`, `REFUND_INVALID`, `EVENT_KEY_INVALID`), `DUPLICATE`. Apply is one transaction: ledger row plus the bounded stock restore (never more than fulfilled minus already restored, and nothing if the old whole-order restore already ran); a blocked event is never written and applying one throws. `OrderExceptionsPanel.cs` (the production entry for cancel/return decisions): SKU / adet / tutar / para birimi inputs with "İade mutabakatı önizle" and "İade mutabakatını uygula", the event key derived from the exception record and the inputs (same inputs twice = duplicate, changed inputs = new event), a full return resolving the record.

Second real defect, found by the seventh test rather than by reading: `OrderExceptionStore` lower-cases the marketplace of every record ("yerel"), `CreateOrderRestockPreview` lower-cases its argument too, but `ApplyOrderStock`/`GetOrderStockStatus` store and look up receipts with the order's own casing ("Yerel") -- so `Reconcile` promised `PreviewReady` for a returned manual order and the panel's very next click threw "daha önce uygulanmış stok hareketi bulunamadı". Reproduced through the real queue (`Reconcile` → `List` → `CreateOrderRestockPreview` with the record's fields) and fixed in `CatalogOrderStock.cs` by matching the marketplace with `COLLATE NOCASE` on the two receipt lookups (shop and order ids stay exact); the new reconciliation uses the same rule. Etsy orders were unaffected only because "etsy" happens to be lowercase already.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ReturnRefundReconciliationSuiteTests.cs` (new, 7 tests, real stores; the file was red before the service existed and the seventh stayed red until the case fix): partial then remainder → `OK_PARTIAL` then `OK_FULL`, stock 7 → 8 → 10; over-return blocked both at once (4 of 3) and cumulatively (2 + 2), restoring nothing and writing nothing; over-refund blocked against the total including refunds already applied, exactly the remainder allowed, negative amount blocked; currency mismatch blocked with nothing recorded, codes compared case-insensitively; a duplicate event key recognised, counted once, stock restored once; another shop's order not found, unknown SKU blocked, an order that never had stock deducted reconciles to `OK_FULL` with zero to restore; and the mixed-case marketplace restock preview.

## #789 — shipping lifecycle integration suite (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-789-shipping-lifecycle-suite off codex/issue-788-return-refund-reconciliation

New issue (TEST). Inventory first: the shipping lifecycle lives in `OrdersStore.Save` (the merge of an API re-save over local observations, and the out-of-order guard `previous.UpdatedAt >= o.UpdatedAt`), `OrderNormalizer.Normalize` (run by `OrdersEtsyClient` on every receipt: state normalization, length limits), `OrdersRules.Validate` (duplicate package ids) and `ShippingLabelCenter.CreatePreview` (duplicate tracking codes). Two things the issue asks to test did not exist: carrier normalization -- the normalizer only trimmed the carrier, so "yurtici", "YURTİÇİ KARGO" and "Yurtiçi Kargo A.Ş." were three carriers in every label and filter -- and any delivery SLA at all.

`OrdersModels.cs`: `OrderNormalizer.NormalizeCarrier` maps the common Turkish and international carriers on a folded form (Turkish-aware lower-casing -- `İ` is handled before `ToLowerInvariant`, which would otherwise turn it into i + combining dot -- diacritics stripped, letters and digits only) and keeps an unknown carrier verbatim rather than guessing; it runs inside `Normalize`, i.e. on the real Etsy read path. `OrdersRules.EvaluateSla(shipment, fallbackShippedAtUtc, nowUtc, maxTransitDays = 5)` returns `DELIVERED` / `RETURNED` / `EXCEPTION` (terminal, never late), `NOT_SHIPPED`, `IN_TRANSIT` or `DELAYED` with the days in transit counted from the first shipped/in-transit observation; an API shipment carries no observations (the Etsy client records none), so the caller passes the receipt's source timestamp as the latest moment the package can have shipped. `OrderSnapshot.SlaLabelAt(now)` rolls that up per order (a delayed package makes the order late, otherwise the longest transit, otherwise delivered/unshipped) and `[JsonIgnore] SlaLabel` feeds a new "Teslimat SLA" column in the orders grid; the shipment editor shows the package's own SLA line. Time-dependent values are never serialized into the payload.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ShippingLifecycleSuiteTests.cs` (new, 6 tests, real `OrdersStore` on disk, times fixed relative to one `Now`; red first on the three missing members): a split shipment keeps both packages with their own carrier, tracking and state and shows every distinct state, and a partial shipment (one delivered, one not yet notified) stays visible as such with no stray separators; carrier normalization on the real read path collapses three spellings into one carrier and keeps an unknown one verbatim; duplicate tracking is blocked at the label preview even across orders/carriers while a code-less package is not a duplicate, and a batch with duplicate package ids is rejected as a whole with nothing written; an older notification arriving after a newer one is ignored, and an API re-save never overwrites a local delivery observation (state, source, tracking URL and the observation history all survive); the SLA evaluator on late / on-time / delivered / unshipped / observation-less packages including the exact-allowance boundary and the invalid allowance; and a saved order re-read by a fresh store instance after a restart reports "Gecikti (8 gün)" for its undelivered package while an all-delivered order reports "Teslim edildi".

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=OrdersModels.cs, OrdersPanel.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ShippingLifecycleSuiteTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=273 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 267); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 1c943029843f5c400a77c8a77b30423d3599b32f9eecb043e2ebd4f323b9ef73.
Not done: the SLA allowance is a constant (5 days) rather than a per-carrier or per-marketplace setting; the Etsy client still records no "shipped" observation for API shipments, so their transit time is bounded by the receipt timestamp instead of measured; no SLA filter in the orders list (the column is sortable, that is all); the carrier table is a short hard-coded list -- unknown carriers pass through unchanged by design and can be added as they appear; the Etsy client's own per-receipt dedupe of packages (`receipt_shipping_id`) is not exercised here because it needs the HTTP fixture, which the existing Etsy safety tests own.

## #791 — end-to-end data integrity regression suite (2026-09-12), branch codex/issue-791-e2e-integrity-suite off codex/issue-789-shipping-lifecycle-suite

New issue (TEST, the umbrella over #787-#790). `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/EndToEndIntegritySuiteTests.cs` (new, 6 tests) runs one deterministic chain through the production entry points on real stores -- supplier feed import (complete feed with hash) → product master → price policy and stock decision → order, shipment observation, return reconciliation → product-order report and dashboard snapshot -- and then re-runs the same chain under the issue's six conditions: happy path with identity and provenance (a second feed with a changed price updates the same product id, never a new one; the price preview carries the cost it priced from; the source names the feed it came from; report and dashboard agree on one order, one applied stock decision, one return), partial failure (a feed failing on its third row lands nothing and the source still points at the last good feed; a blocked over-return changes nothing downstream), restart (fresh store instances after the pools are cleared find the policy, mapping, observation history, receipt and return ledger), duplicate input at every step (same feed hash → `AlreadyApplied`, second stock decision deducts nothing, same return event → `DUPLICATE`, same order snapshot re-saved → ignored), wrong-store access (shop B finds, sees, prices and reconciles nothing of shop A), and redaction (secrets entering through a sync error, an exception message and a product name never come back out of `LastError`, the exception record or the search index).

The first run found two things. One was a real leak: `SyncStore.Redact` replaced the *names* of secrets ("access_token" → "[redacted]") and left their *values* intact, so a failed price job stored `Authorization: Bearer <token>` verbatim in `LastError` -- which the dashboard notifications and the sync panel display; the token value in the test survived only mangled because it happened to contain the word "secret". Fixed by running the central `AuditStore.Sanitize` (bearer headers, key=value secrets, PII) before the name replacements. The other was a contradiction rather than a bug in the chain: the orders screen promised "İşlenen ürünün XML stok kilidi etkinleştirilir; böylece tedarikçi güncellemesi satılan stoğu geri yazmaz", but nothing ever sets `LockStock` after a sale -- `ApplyOrderStock` deliberately treats the movement as transient ("do not turn it into the user's manual supplier-lock"), and the next complete feed writes the supplier's stock back (10 → 8 sold → 9 returned → 10 from the feed). For dropshipping the supplier's count *is* the truth and a permanent lock would silently stop all future stock updates for that product, so the behaviour is kept and the two UI texts now say what actually happens (the movement is local and transient; the product card's stock lock is the permanent control), and the suite asserts the real semantics with the receipt and return ledger keeping the history. A third, smaller inconsistency surfaced on the manual path: `SaveManual` did not run the #789 carrier normalization, so "aras" typed by the operator and "Aras Kargo" from Etsy were two carriers; it now does.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=Catalog/SyncStore.cs, OrdersStore.cs, OrdersPanel.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/EndToEndIntegritySuiteTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=279 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 273); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 40a746b9e4527dec5afa1beaf4417ac584d3384110c615c85febf1c068ccacff.
Not done: the chain uses manual ("Yerel") orders, not an Etsy pull -- the HTTP path stays with the Etsy safety tests; the second feed reports every known row as "updated" because the first import does not stamp `PriceSource`/`StockSource`/`SourceKind` while updates do (a counting quirk in `ImportSummary`, recorded, not changed); local sales are still overwritten by the next supplier feed by design -- a reservation model (supplier stock minus open local movements since the last feed) would be the real answer to overselling and is its own design issue; `OrderExceptionStore` redacts with `MarketplaceConnectionStore.Redact` rather than the central sanitizer (it covers headers and key=value tokens, not PII) -- not unified here.

## #792 — product list column layout persistence (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-792-product-list-layout-persistence off codex/issue-791-e2e-integrity-suite

New issue, first of the DESIGN block. ENTRY is the product list load/save layout in `MainWindow`. What existed persisted only the *hidden* column headers, under `columns:products`, and applied them from `AddProductFilters` -- which `BuildProducts` calls **before** it adds a single column, so the loop ran over an empty collection and nothing was ever restored after a restart. Width, order and the sort choice were not persisted at all.

`DataGridLayoutState.cs` (new, no WPF dependency): `DataGridLayoutState`/`DataGridColumnLayout` plus `DataGridLayoutCodec` with the persistence format and, more importantly, the trust and fallback rules. Columns are keyed by *binding path*, not header text, so a renamed or translated header keeps its layout; widths are device-independent pixels, which is what makes a layout saved at 100% DPI lay out identically at 150% and 200% (WPF scales DIPs itself -- storing `ActualWidth` in pixels would have shrunk every column at high DPI). Anything untrustworthy -- empty, not JSON, another schema version, no columns -- deserializes to null and the caller keeps the defaults, while an individual entry with no key or an impossible width is dropped without discarding the rest. `ResolveOrder` is the order fallback: persisted keys in their saved order, a saved key that no longer exists simply skipped, and every column the layout has never seen inserted at *its own default index* rather than appended, so a column added by a newer version appears where the code puts it.

`MainWindow.Filters.cs`: `InitializeProductLayout` (called from `BuildProducts` once the columns exist) applies the stored layout and then subscribes -- `ColumnDisplayIndexChanged`, `ColumnReordered`, and a `DependencyPropertyDescriptor` per column for `Width` and `Visibility`, plus `Closing` and the filter bar's apply -- with an `applyingProductLayout` guard so restoring never writes back. The sort controls are exposed to the layout code through two delegates set where they are created, so the sort travels with the layout and lands in both the combo box and `productFilter`. The column chooser now writes through `SaveProductLayout` as well. The legacy `ApplyProductColumnPreferences` stays as the fallback for a database that only has the old hidden-header list, so an existing install keeps its hidden columns on first run and gets the full layout from then on. Scope: the key lives in the store's own `ui-preferences.db`, so a different data directory is a different layout by construction.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DataGridLayoutCodecTests.cs` (new, 3) covers the format round-trip with DIP widths, every untrustworthy input, and the order resolution including removed columns, new columns in the middle, and two saved columns claiming the same index. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductListLayoutPersistenceTests.cs` (new, 3, real `MainWindow` on an STA thread, red first): order, width, visibility and sort survive a restart (a second `MainWindow` over the same directory); a layout naming a removed "Ghost" column applies the rest with the saved relative order while unknown columns keep their default positions, and a corrupt layout falls back to the defaults; and a layout saved for one store is not applied to another. Two of my own expectations were wrong about where an unknown column lands and were corrected to the documented rule -- the implementation was right, the test was guessing.

## #793 — product list density switch (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-793-product-list-density off codex/issue-792-product-list-layout-persistence

New issue. ENTRY is the product list view settings. The requirement that matters is "aynı DataGrid/style owner üzerinde" -- one control, one style owner, no parallel UI -- and that font, row height, thumbnail and hit target scale *together*, which is exactly what goes wrong when density is bolted on as a row-height setter and the text stays the same size in a squashed row.

`ProductListDensity.cs` (new): the two modes as one metrics table (`RowHeight`, `FontSize`, `ThumbnailSize`, `CellPadding`) so the four numbers can never drift apart in separate styles, plus `Normalize` (unknown input falls back to comfortable rather than to an empty style; accepts the Turkish label back) and `Label`. Compact is bounded by usability rather than by how small it can go: 28 DIP rows are still a comfortable pointer target and 12 DIP text is still legible, and because these are device-independent units WPF scales them itself at 150%/200% DPI. `MainWindow.Filters.cs`: `BuildProductDensitySelector` adds one combo box to the existing product toolbar, restores the stored mode, and on change calls `ApplyProductDensity` -- which sets `RowHeight`/`MinRowHeight`/`FontSize` and a `CellStyle` carrying the mode's padding on the *same* `products` grid -- then persists under `density:products` in the store's `ui-preferences.db`, next to the #792 layout. Nothing rebinds `ItemsSource`, which is what keeps the selection and the recycling virtualizer untouched.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductListDensityTests.cs` (new, 2; written first, red as a compile failure against the missing selector and metrics). The first test pins the metric relationships rather than the literals -- compact is denser in every dimension, the row never goes below 24 DIP, text never below 11, a thumbnail is never taller than its row, row height stays proportional to the font, and an unknown mode falls back -- so a future tweak to the numbers stays honest. The second runs the real `MainWindow`: select a row, switch to compact, and the selection survives while the live grid's `RowHeight` and `FontSize` match the compact metrics and `EnableRowVirtualization` / `IsVirtualizing` / `VirtualizationMode.Recycling` are all still in place; then a second `MainWindow` over the same directory comes up compact.

## #794 — product row state hierarchy (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-794-product-row-state-hierarchy off codex/issue-793-product-list-density

New issue. ENTRY is the product row template. The list showed one thing about a row's condition -- a "Durum" column reading Aktif/Pasif from `StatusLabel` -- while the fields that actually decide whether an operator can do anything with the row (`Duplicate`, `SourceMissing`, `Anomaly`, an attempted Etsy dispatch with no listing id, a feed that has gone quiet) were invisible unless you opened the card. There was no hierarchy to speak of, so the first job was to define one rather than to paint states.

`ProductRowState.cs` (new): `Classify(product, nowUtc)` reads only the product's own persisted fields -- no second source of truth -- and resolves the six states by one documented precedence: **disabled** (nothing else matters if the row cannot sell) > **error** (duplicate identity or a missing source record) > **warning** (feed anomaly) > **pending** (dispatch attempted, no listing id back) > **stale** (a feed-backed product untouched by its source for more than seven days; a `manual` product is never "stale", it is simply as old as the operator left it) > normal. The precedence picks the badge and the border, and *every* condition that also applies is listed in the reason, so nothing is hidden by the ranking. Each state carries a glyph, a word and a border weight, so the row reads on a monochrome display; `AccentBrush(key, highContrast)` puts colour last and abandons the app palette for `SystemColors` under high contrast -- a hand-picked amber is exactly what a high-contrast theme exists to override. `Tooltip` gives the state and the reason plus stock, deliberately not the buying cost, the margin or the source identity (a hover tooltip is the easiest thing to catch in a screenshot or a screen share), sanitized through `AuditStore.Sanitize` and capped at 400 characters so a pathological product name cannot turn it into a wall of text.

Wiring: the "Durum" column now binds the whole row through `ProductRowStateBadgeConverter` to the badge (glyph + word), and `InitializeProductRowStates` hooks `LoadingRow` -- the one event that also fires when the recycling virtualizer *reuses* a row, so a scrolled list can never leave a previous product's border or tooltip behind -- to set the border thickness and brush, the tooltip, and `AutomationProperties.ItemStatus` so a screen reader announces the state too.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductRowStateTests.cs` (new, 4, red first): each state classified from its own field, including the two negative cases (a listing id clears pending; a manual product is not stale); combined states resolving by precedence with the other conditions still named in the reason, plus a check that severity is monotonic in that order; every state distinct by badge alone with a glyph, a word and a ≥3 DIP border, and high contrast changing the colour while leaving the non-colour signals identical; and the tooltip containing the state but never the cost, never the source id, never a bearer token, and never more than 400 characters.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=ProductRowState.cs (new), MainWindow.Filters.cs, MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductRowStateTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=291 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 287); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 6f4175ae3c901e7f24a00999a78a42604d4e9f137ce045c5cff1663d493dc975.
## #795 — product list selection summary bar (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-795-selection-summary-bar off codex/issue-794-product-row-state-hierarchy

New issue. ENTRY is the product list's selection-changed event. The list already had three bulk actions in the toolbar ("Aktife al", "Pasife al", "Ürünü sil") and nothing that told the operator what they were about to act on: no count, no scope, no warning that some of the selection cannot be deleted, and no way to clear a selection except ctrl-clicking rows back off. Worse, the selection survives a filter change, so a row that has scrolled out of the filter is still in `SelectedItems` and would have been acted on unseen.

`ProductSelectionSummary.cs` (new, pure): `Describe(selected, visible, matchTotal)` reconciles the selection against the rows *currently in the list* -- anything no longer there is counted as stale, excluded from `LiveIds`, and stated in the scope text rather than silently acted on -- reports the live count against the full match count (so selecting a page is never mistaken for selecting the whole filter), and computes risky-action availability from the set rather than always offering it: "activate" is only enabled when something in the selection is actually passive, and the deletable/blocked split mirrors `CatalogStore.DeleteProduct`'s refusal of anything with an Etsy listing or a dispatch attempt, so the bar says "2 ürün silinemez" before the operator meets that error one dialog at a time. Only counts are rendered -- no product text -- which is what keeps a name or a cost from travelling into the bar.

`MainWindow`: `BuildProductSelectionBar` is a sticky `Border` docked between the toolbar and the grid (new three-part `Dock` overload), collapsed while nothing is selected, carrying the headline, the scope/risk detail and a "Seçimi temizle" button, with `AutomationProperties` name and help text so the bar is announced rather than being a visual-only affordance. It updates from its own `SelectionChanged` subscription -- separate from the existing edit-resolution handler, so the summary is correct regardless of which path that takes.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductSelectionSummaryTests.cs` (new, 4, red first): 0 / 1 / 1000 selected rows each with their own headline and a scope naming both the selection and the 5.000 matches behind it; a selection left behind by a filter change reported as stale, excluded from `LiveIds`, and -- when *every* selected row is gone -- collapsing the bar rather than offering actions on nothing; risky-action availability across a mixed set (activate off when everything is already active, 2 deletable vs 2 blocked, all-blocked disabling delete entirely); and a product whose name carries a bearer token and a four-figure cost, with every string the bar renders asserted free of both.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=ProductSelectionSummary.cs (new), MainWindow.Filters.cs, MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductSelectionSummaryTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=295 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 291); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 f1da8cad29dec6fdac6f41ed3cac8969bcaf8c57b99933d1a0ca06c8dfc547e1.
## #796 — product quick-inspect drawer (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-796-product-quick-inspect-drawer off codex/issue-795-selection-summary-bar

New issue. ENTRY is the product row's inspect command. Checking why a row is in the state it is meant opening the editor -- which loads the whole card, arms the unsaved-changes machinery, and takes the operator off the row they were scanning. The issue asks for a read-only drawer instead, and is explicit about two constraints: no mutation controls, and no PII or secrets.

`ProductQuickInspect.cs` (new, pure): `Build(product, jobsForThatProduct, nowUtc)` returns a flat list of section/label/value rows covering the five things the issue names -- identity, price, stock, source, readiness, and the last error. Read-only is a property of the *shape* rather than a rule someone has to remember: the model holds strings, so there is nothing a drawer could two-way bind to. Readiness is derived from what the product actually lacks (SKU or barcode, name, sale price, description, image), so "Eksik (3)" lists the three; the last error is the most recent **failed** sync job for that product id, which a later success does not erase. Missing data renders as an em dash rather than a blank row or an epoch date, and every value goes through `AuditStore.Sanitize` plus a 300-character cap -- product names and supplier descriptions are free text that has, in practice, carried tokens and customer contact details.

`MainWindow`: `BuildProductInspectHost` wraps the grid in a two-column host with a 320 DIP drawer that stays collapsed until asked for, opened with Ctrl+I on the selected row and closed with Esc (both as `KeyBinding`s on the grid, plus Esc on the drawer itself and a "Kapat (Esc)" button), moving focus into the drawer on open and back to the grid on close, with an `AutomationProperties` name so it is announced. It rebuilds its content on every open, so reopening after a refresh shows current values rather than the first snapshot.

Tests, red first. `ProductQuickInspectTests.cs` (4): all six sections present with the right values and the most recent failure winning over an older success; readiness derived from the missing fields; missing data as em dashes including a product no feed ever touched; and a product whose name carries a bearer token, whose description carries an email and a phone number, and whose sync error carries an access token -- none of which appear in any row. `ProductQuickInspectDrawerTests.cs` (2, real `MainWindow`): the drawer opens on the command with the selected row's identity, exposes **zero** `TextBox`/`CheckBox`/`ComboBox`/`DataGrid` and exactly one button (close) -- read-only verified against the realized visual tree, not just asserted about the model -- closes again, and does the same through the Ctrl+I and Esc key bindings; and reopening after the stock changed shows 41 where the first open showed 3.

## #797 — product card media fallback states (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-797-media-fallback-states off codex/issue-796-product-quick-inspect-drawer

New issue. ENTRY is the product image presenter, which is `MediaPanel`'s preview. It had one `Image` with a fixed 360×260 and no states at all: selecting a record cleared the source and left an empty area while the fetch ran, a missing record looked identical to a failed one, and a failure wrote `"Önizleme yüklenemedi: " + error.Message` into the status line -- the transport's own message, which for an image fetch is the request URL, signature and all.

`ProductMediaPresentation.cs` (new, pure): the five presentation states (`Loading`, `Missing`, `Broken`, `Ready`, `Cached`) as one shared description with a glyph, a word and a hint, and -- the point of the issue -- one `BoxSize` for all of them, so the placeholder reserves exactly the space the picture will take and the card cannot jump. `Classify` maps a `ProductMediaRecord` plus the load outcome onto those: no record or no URL is *missing* rather than broken, every `MediaStatus` failure (`NotFound`, `Timeout`, `InvalidUrl`, `TooLarge`, `RateLimited`, `UnsupportedFormat`, `Error`) is *broken*, a record the store believes is fine but that would not decode is broken too, and a local file resolves as *cached* because it is not a fresh remote fetch. `SafeSourceLabel` reduces a URL to its host and nothing else -- no path, no query, no embedded credentials -- and a `file:` URL becomes "yerel dosya" rather than naming somebody's home folder. `FailureText` composes the operator-facing line from the status and that host, and deliberately ignores the raw exception text.

`MediaPanel.cs`: the preview is now an `Image` and a placeholder stack inside one fixed-size `Border`, switched by `ShowMediaState`, which also sets the box's automation name so the state is announced. The fetch reports loading before it starts, ready (or cached) on success, and broken on the narrowed set of transport exceptions, with the status line coming from `FailureText`.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductMediaPresentationTests.cs` (new, 4, red first): the four states classified from the record and the load outcome including every failure status; all five states reporting an identical box size and each carrying a distinct word (loading ≠ missing ≠ broken, which is exactly what a single grey box gets wrong); the URL reduced to a host with the signature, the credentials and the path all gone and a local path never named; and a failure line that says "bulunamadı" and the host but never the token or the query.
