## #798 — product card price information hierarchy (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-798-price-information-hierarchy off codex/issue-797-media-fallback-states

New issue. ENTRY is the product card's pricing summary, which did not exist: the card opened with "Alış fiyatı", "Satış fiyatı", "Satış para birimi" and "KDV" as four equally weighted text boxes, so nothing led, the last calculation time was invisible, and a price sold below cost looked exactly like a healthy one. The issue is explicit that no new pricing feature belongs here, which settles the design question: everything shown is derived from figures already on the product, and the authoritative decision stays the dispatch-time money preflight (#285) and the operator's minimum-margin guard (#790).

`ProductPriceSummary.cs` (new, pure): `Build(product, nowUtc)` returns the ranked block -- sale price, then approximate margin, then when it was last calculated -- with the price and its currency in *separate* fields (never concatenated), which is what stops a long or odd currency label from deforming the line; the label itself is clamped. A missing price is an em dash and a warning rather than a rendered zero, and it yields `Unknown` margin rather than a -100%. Margin is only computed when the cost and sale currencies match -- subtracting a USD cost from a TRY price is not a margin, and saying so is the honest answer -- and is classified negative / thin (under 10%) / healthy, each with a warning in the operator's words. Staleness reuses the money calculator's own 24-hour window so the card and the gate agree on what "stale" means, and flagging it never alters the number displayed. Every string carries the caveat that the figure is approximate and that commission, shipping and tax are computed at dispatch.

`MainWindow`: `ShowProductPriceSummary` renders that block at the top of the product card (called from `BindProductEdit`, so it follows the selection), with the price as the one large number, the margin coloured by level *and* labelled, the calculation time small beneath, warnings listed, and an automation name that reads the price and margin together. It is read-only -- no control in the block writes anything, and there is no live marketplace call anywhere in it.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductPriceSummaryTests.cs` (new, 5, red first): the ranked order and healthy case; a missing price as an em dash with `Unknown` margin and a warning; a stale calculation flagged (and a 20-hour-old one not) without changing the price; negative and thin margins warned about, plus mixed currencies refusing to guess; and a ten-character currency code clamped while the price keeps its own field. One assertion of mine was wrong about case (`Yaklaşık` vs `yaklaşık`) and was made case-insensitive rather than bending the copy.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=ProductPriceSummary.cs (new), MainWindow.Filters.cs, MainWindow.ProductEditing.cs, MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductPriceSummaryTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=310 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 305); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 ea05e8f7d3e34e3aea44d9f06e483fdbf9630f652c3c6c66e763167ef8f48ad3.
## #799 — product card stock status composition (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-799-stock-status-composition off codex/issue-798-price-information-hierarchy

New issue. ENTRY is the product card's stock summary, which was a single editable "Stok" box: the number on the card was the on-hand figure, while what a channel would actually be sent is `min(stock − safety buffer, maximum cap)` and could be a completely different number -- or zero -- with nothing on the card saying so. The issue's edge note is the design constraint: *do not copy the calculation owner, use the existing stock projection*.

`ProductStockSummary.cs` (new, pure): `Build(product, policy, projectedAvailable, nowUtc)` composes on-hand, the projected available quantity, the safety buffer, the maximum cap, how much is withheld, and the age of the figure. The available quantity is **passed in**, never computed -- `CatalogStore.PreviewStock` owns that projection, and a second implementation here is precisely how a card ends up contradicting the dispatcher. "Withheld" is likewise stated as the observed difference between on-hand and the projection rather than re-derived from buffer and cap, so a projection rule this card does not know about still adds up. Levels (`Ready`, `BufferOnly`, `Capped`, `OutOfStock`, `Invalid`, `NoProjection`) each carry a glyph and a distinct word, so the composition reads without colour; a negative stock is `Invalid` with a warning rather than displayed as sellable; and with no saved policy there is no projection, which the card says outright instead of showing on-hand stock as if it were available. Staleness reuses `ProductRowState.StaleAfter`, so "stale" means one thing across the app, and a manually maintained product is never stale.

`MainWindow`: `ShowProductStockSummary` (called from `BindProductEdit`) reads the policy and asks `PreviewStock` for the projection -- catching the "no policy / policy disabled" `InvalidOperationException` as *no projection* rather than as an error -- and renders the level, the three quantities, the buffer/cap/age line, and any warnings above the editable stock field, with an automation name that reads the level and both quantities.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductStockSummaryTests.cs` (new, 6, red first). The first one is the point of the issue: given a projection that deliberately disagrees with the naive arithmetic, the summary reports the projection -- if it recomputed, it would say 7 and the card would lie. Then: no projection saying so rather than inventing a number; zero, buffer-only and negative each reading differently; every level distinct by word with a stale feed called out and the quantity unchanged by the flag; large quantities grouped and kept in separate fields; and a final test that runs the **real** `CatalogStore` -- import, `SaveStockPolicy(safety 3, cap 5)`, `PreviewStock` -- and asserts the card shows the store's own 5 and reads it as capped.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=ProductStockSummary.cs (new), MainWindow.Filters.cs, MainWindow.ProductEditing.cs, MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductStockSummaryTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=316 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 310); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 26587b010ad7e1f6868df5b00d822c5aa30bd2d166b35ba4ae4d4f6329929e2a.
## #800 — product card source provenance affordance (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-800-source-provenance-affordance off codex/issue-799-stock-status-composition

New issue. ENTRY is the product card's provenance command. The card exposed the raw per-field markers as four more columns in the grid (`SourceKind`, `PriceSource`, `StockSource`, `MediaSource`) and nothing on the card itself, so "where did this price come from, and is that source's mapping still the one that wrote it?" had no answer short of reading the XML source screen.

`ProductProvenance.cs` (new, pure): `Build(product, source, nowUtc)` turns the markers the import already maintains into one row per field (Fiyat, Stok, Görseller, Başlık, Açıklama), each naming its origin -- the source's name, or "Elle girildi" for an operator-set field -- and saying what that means next time a feed runs, which is where the lock flags belong: a locked field is precisely the reason the import will *not* take it back. The header is the one-line answer ("3 alan kaynaktan, 2 alan elle girildi"), and `SourceSummary` carries the source and the mapping revision that actually wrote these values; when the source's `MappingRevision` has moved past `LastAppliedMappingRevision`, that is flagged as a warning because the field origins may no longer describe what a fresh import would produce. A product whose source cannot be resolved says so instead of showing an empty summary, and a product that never came from a feed reads "Elle oluşturuldu" rather than being reported as missing a source.

The security rule is enforced by shape: the summary takes the source's **name** and nothing else. `XmlSource.Location` is the feed URL, which in this app routinely carries a key in its query string, so it is never passed in -- and the test asserts that not even the host appears, since a provenance line has no use for it.

`MainWindow`: `ShowProductProvenance` (from `BindProductEdit`) fills a collapsed `Expander` on the card -- the issue's "kartı kalabalıklaştırma" -- whose header alone carries the summary line, so the detail is one keyboard-reachable expand away and costs nothing when closed.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductProvenanceTests.cs` (new, 5, red first): each field naming its source with operator overrides and their locks called out; a mapping revision that has moved on flagged with both numbers; an unresolvable source and a never-fed product distinguished; mixed origins summarized without hiding either side; and a source whose location carries `user:pw@` and `?key=abc123secret` producing a summary that contains the source name and none of that, not even the host.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=ProductProvenance.cs (new), MainWindow.Filters.cs, MainWindow.ProductEditing.cs, MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductProvenanceTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=321 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 316); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 9a7b701bc1267546bfefb498dbc8f60fa1e9c65654dbf4be196f4eb628faf036.
## #801 — product workspace section navigation (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-801-workspace-section-navigation off codex/issue-800-source-provenance-affordance

New issue. ENTRY is the product workspace's navigation. It was a five-tab `TabControl` whose sections were addressed nowhere but by position -- no key, no deep link, and no memory: every return to the workspace dropped the operator back on the first tab. The tab set did not match the six sections the issue names either: description and images shared one tab, there was no price/stock section, and a "XML / provenance" tab duplicated what #800 had just put on the card.

`ProductWorkspaceSections.cs` (new, pure): the six sections as one catalogue -- `identity`, `content`, `price-stock`, `media`, `channel`, `audit` -- with stable keys (the deep-link surface) separate from labels (display text, freely translatable). `Resolve` matches case-insensitively and trimmed, and a key this build does not have resolves to the default section **and reports `Found = false`**, so a caller can say the link was stale instead of quietly showing the wrong pane. `Route`/`ParseRoute` are the `products#section` link format, round-tripping for every catalogued section. `ProductWorkspaceSectionMemory` is the focus-restore rule in one place: an explicit deep link wins and becomes the remembered section, navigating without one keeps the operator where they were, and an unknown key never overwrites a good remembered section.

`MainWindow`: the workspace is rebuilt as those six tabs, each tagged with its catalogue key, in the catalogue's order; `SelectProductSection` resolves through the memory, selects the tab and moves keyboard focus into its content (the focus-restore half of the issue); and the tab strip writes back to the memory on user selection.

The restructure broke two existing tests, and both were right to break: they addressed tabs by *index*, which is the coupling this issue exists to remove. `ProductCardKeyboardSmokeTests` now asserts the workspace exposes exactly the catalogued keys in the catalogue's order -- which immediately caught that my tab order had `media` before `price-stock` while the issue (and the catalogue) list price/stock first, so the tabs were reordered rather than the catalogue bent. `ProductCardDirtyDraftTests` now looks sections up by key and searches the section's subtree for its fields, because a body may nest fields under headings; it previously assumed they were direct children.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductWorkspaceSectionsTests.cs` (new, 4, red first): the catalogue's stability, order and uniqueness; known keys resolving and unknown ones falling back while reporting it; deep links round-tripping for every section with a bare page link carrying no section; and the memory restoring the last section, ignoring an unknown key, and letting an explicit link win.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=ProductWorkspaceSections.cs (new), MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductWorkspaceSectionsTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductCardKeyboardSmokeTests.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductCardDirtyDraftTests.cs, IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=325 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 321); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 715cd62a76a1f0e53a7bc2638d720dcf3bf46808a1f137a1f644f3f4c67828fc.
## #802 — product workspace unsaved-change indicator (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-802-unsaved-change-indicator off codex/issue-801-workspace-section-navigation

New issue. The dirty-state *guard* already existed and works (`ProductEditIsDirty` plus the Kaydet/Vazgeç/İptal prompt on selection change). What it lacked was any visibility: an operator with unsaved work saw nothing until they tried to leave, could not tell **which** part was unsaved, and had only an all-or-nothing discard.

`ProductDirtySections.cs` (new, pure): every writable `CatalogProduct` property is mapped to one of the #801 workspace sections and to an operator-facing field name. `Compare(baseline, current)` reports the dirty sections in the workspace's own order with the changed field **names** -- values are serialized only to detect the difference and never leave the method, because a banner reading "Alış fiyatı: 1234,56 → 4321,99" puts the cost on any shared screen. `ResetSection` is the per-section undo: it restores that section's properties from the baseline and leaves every other section's unsaved work intact. `UnmappedEditableProperties` exists so a test can fail the day someone adds a property with no section -- otherwise a change to it would silently show no indicator at all.

`MainWindow`: `RefreshProductDirtyIndicator` marks each dirty section's tab with a dot, shows a summary line ("Kaydedilmemiş değişiklik: 2 bölüm, 3 alan") and one row per section listing its changed field names with a "Geri al" button wired to `ResetProductSection`; `AutomationProperties` carries the summary. It refreshes on binding (via `BindProductEdit`) and on `LostKeyboardFocus` within the card, which is when WPF's default `LostFocus` bindings actually write back to the product.

Tests, red first. `ProductDirtySectionsTests.cs` (6): a clean product has nothing; each field attributed to its own section; many dirty sections listed in workspace order with a counted summary; the leak test (a four-figure cost, a four-figure price, an email in a description -- none of which may appear anywhere in the rendered indicator, while the field names must); section reset restoring only its own fields and leaving the rest; and the completeness check that no editable property is unmapped. `ProductDirtyIndicatorUiTests.cs` (1, real `MainWindow`): editing the price marks the price/stock tab and not the content tab, the bar names "Satış fiyatı" and does not contain "250", a second edit adds a second section, and clicking that section's "Geri al" restores the price to 10 while the description edit survives.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=ProductDirtySections.cs (new), MainWindow.Filters.cs, MainWindow.ProductEditing.cs, MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductDirtySectionsTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductDirtyIndicatorUiTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=332 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 325); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 bb5e582e1c6e00e8d98d1e5985c631ade53114e1f1bfdc3d1bc1b36c0c2d676a.
## #803 — product workspace validation summary panel (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-803-validation-summary-panel off codex/issue-802-unsaved-change-indicator

New issue, and the follow-up #802 flagged as not done. Validation existed only as a refusal: `CatalogStore.Valid` threw one lumped message ("Ürün adı, kimliği, fiyatı, stoku veya KDV oranı geçersiz.") at save time, so an operator with three problems learned about one of them, in words that did not say which field, with no way to reach it.

`ProductValidation.cs` (new): one evaluator returning findings as (severity, section, field, message), ordered blocking → warning → info and then by the #801 workspace section order, which is what makes "the first blocker" a meaningful thing to link to (`FirstBlockingRoute` is a `products#section` deep link straight to it). Blocking is exactly what the store refuses; warnings are saveable-but-not-listable gaps (no price, no description, no image, active with zero stock); info is context (locked fields). Messages name the field and the rule -- a limit, a required value -- and never quote the offending content, because product text has carried tokens and customer addresses before.

The part that matters more than the panel: **`CatalogStore.Valid` now delegates to that evaluator** (`ProductValidation.BlockingMessage`). Writing the rules twice is how a panel ends up promising a save the store rejects, or refusing one it would have accepted; now there is one owner, and the store's messages became specific as a side effect ("KDV oranı 0 ile 100 arasında olmalı." instead of the lumped sentence). `Valid` keeps its own null guard, since the evaluator reads those properties.

`MainWindow`: `ShowProductValidation` renders the summary at the top of the card -- a headline with the counts, an "İlk soruna git" button wired to `SelectProductSection`, and the findings grouped under their section labels -- refreshed on binding and on every dirty-indicator refresh, and hidden entirely when there is nothing actionable.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductValidationSummaryTests.cs` (new, 5, red first): a clean product with no blockers and an empty route; three blockers grouped by section with the first one deep-linked and ordered by the workspace's own order; missing description/image reported as warnings rather than errors; a 900-character name, a description carrying a bearer token and an email, and a missing SKU producing findings that name the fields and quote none of the values; and -- the equivalence test that justifies the refactor -- four blocking cases each asserted to be *both* reported by the evaluator *and* actually refused by `CatalogStore.SaveProduct`, plus a passing product that really saves.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=ProductValidation.cs (new), Catalog/CatalogStore.cs, MainWindow.Filters.cs, MainWindow.ProductEditing.cs, MainWindow.xaml.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductValidationSummaryTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=337 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 332); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 8c4d605afbc6b6f218726f3b5e39a1036768c4aa7d3c9e16747496be5526d9f3.
## #804 — product media gallery keyboard navigation (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-804-media-gallery-keyboard off codex/issue-803-validation-summary-panel

New issue. ENTRY is the product media gallery, which did not exist: the media screen listed records in a `DataGrid` (with the full URL in a column) beside the single preview box from #797. There was no thumbnail strip, so "look through this product's images" meant arrowing a table row by row, and nothing summarized what was selected.

`MediaGalleryNavigation.cs` (new, pure): the movement rules -- Left/Up back one, Right/Down forward one, Home/End to the ends, everything else leaving the selection alone -- with the edges decided once rather than inside a key handler. The strip **stops at its ends instead of wrapping**, because wrapping surprises someone scanning left to right; an index left over from a longer gallery is clamped rather than trusted; an empty gallery stays at "nothing selected"; and the first key press on an unselected gallery picks the first image. `Summary` names position, state and host (reusing #797's wording and its host-only URL rule) and is the line an operator screenshots, which is exactly why it never carries the URL.

A real leak surfaced while wiring it: the media `DataGrid` shows the full URL in a column and, with WPF's default `ClipboardCopyMode`, **Ctrl+C copied whole rows -- signed supplier URLs and all -- onto the clipboard**, silently. That is the "remote URL clipboard leakage" the issue names. Implicit copy is now off (`DataGridClipboardCopyMode.None`), and copying an address is a deliberate, labelled act: a "Görsel adresini kopyala" button that copies the selected image's URL and only that. `ExplicitCopyValue` is where that distinction is written down and tested against `ClipboardRedaction`, which is what any *automatic* copy still goes through.

`MediaPanel.cs`: a horizontal, virtualizing `ListBox` strip whose tiles carry the state glyph, the host and the position (never the URL), keyboard flow through the shared rules with focus moved onto the tile so it is visibly focused, `ScrollIntoView` so keyboard movement does not scroll off-screen, Enter/Space triggering the copy action, a summary line beneath, and `AutomationProperties` carrying that summary. Selecting in the strip drives the same selection and preview the grid does.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/MediaGalleryNavigationTests.cs` (new, 5, red first): arrows stepping and stopping at both ends with Home/End and an unowned key; one-image and empty galleries plus a stale index; Enter/Space as activation; the summary naming position, state, host and "Ana görsel" while containing neither the signature nor the path nor embedded credentials, and still summarizing a record with no URL; and the copy distinction -- redacted for the implicit path, verbatim for the operator's explicit one.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=MediaGalleryNavigation.cs (new), MediaPanel.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/MediaGalleryNavigationTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=342 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 337); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 42d9b7d3554dbf534ce7586738d1af6efcbf1f736d1749234bbea5f8a0c6dd59.
## #805 — product content before-after preview (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-805-content-before-after-preview off codex/issue-804-media-gallery-keyboard

New issue. ENTRY is the save preview. #802 made it visible *that* content had changed and #803 whether it was valid, but the operator still could not see *what* the customer-facing text would become before committing it -- which matters most for the description, the one field a supplier feed rewrites wholesale.

`ProductContentDiff.cs` (new, pure): `Build(baseline, current, stored)` returns one row per changed content field (title, description, brand, category) with the before and after values. This is deliberately the opposite rule from #802's indicator: that one is always on screen and therefore names fields only, while this one is opened on purpose to see the values -- so the values are shown, but sanitized through `AuditStore.Sanitize`, flattened to a single line (a description carries CRLFs and markup) and capped at 400 characters with a visible ellipsis, so neither a 4 KB HTML blob nor a token pasted in from a feed can fill or leak through the dialog. An emptied field reads "(boş)" rather than as a blank line, and unchanged fields are left out entirely. Passing the currently persisted product marks the preview stale when the record has moved on under the operator, because a diff against a baseline that no longer exists describes a save that will not happen.

`MainWindow`: an "İçerik değişikliklerini önizle" button opens a read-only dialog listing each field's before and after with the staleness warning at the top. The dialog contains no control except Close -- nothing in this path writes anything, locally or to a marketplace.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductContentDiffTests.cs` (new, 6, red first): an unchanged product previewing nothing and saying so; only the changed fields appearing, in a fixed order, with their before/after values; emptying and filling a field reading as "(boş)" on the right side; a 4 KB HTML description flattened, capped and visibly truncated; a stale baseline detected and warned about; and a description carrying a bearer token and an email producing a preview with neither.

## #806 — product audit history readability (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-806-audit-history-readability off codex/issue-805-content-before-after-preview

New issue, and the last of the product block. ENTRY is the product audit tab, which showed the order report and three source fields -- the audit store's events were reachable only from the global diagnostics screen, unfiltered, as raw rows.

`ProductAuditTimeline.cs` (new, pure): `Build(events, productId, nowUtc)` filters to the product, orders newest-first, and -- the readability decision -- collapses **consecutive** events sharing action, module and outcome into one entry carrying the attempt count and the span between the first and last try. Retries are what floods this list; twelve identical 429s are one thing that happened, and the span is what tells an operator whether it is still going. A different outcome breaks the run, so a success after a string of failures is never swallowed into them. The list is capped at 200 entries (keeping the newest) with the headline saying so, and each entry's detail -- the expandable part -- is re-sanitized through `AuditStore.Sanitize` and capped here regardless of what the writer did, because an audit row is exactly where a failed call's message and its token end up.

`MainWindow`: `ShowProductAuditTimeline` (from `BindProductEdit`) renders it into the workspace's "Geçmiş" section, each entry a one-line summary with its detail behind an `Expander` -- keyboard-reachable, collapsed by default -- and a read failure degrading to a message rather than taking the card down.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/ProductAuditTimelineTests.cs` (new, 6, red first): an empty history stating itself; only this product's events, newest first; twelve identical retries collapsing to one entry with the count and the 11-minute span; a success and a second action breaking the grouping into 1/2/1; five hundred events capped at 200 keeping the newest and staying strictly ordered; and a detail carrying an access token, a bearer header, an email and 3 KB of padding coming back redacted and capped, with `HasDetail` false when there was nothing to expand. One of my own assertions used ungrammatical Turkish ("kayıt yok" for "kaydı yok") and was corrected rather than bending the copy.

## #807 — dashboard KPI freshness indicators (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-807-kpi-freshness-indicators off codex/issue-806-audit-history-readability

New issue, first of the dashboard block. ENTRY is the KPI render. Six cards showed a number and nothing else: no data time, no coverage, and -- the failure that matters -- **a refresh that threw left the previous figures on screen looking exactly as current as before**, with only a red line in the status bar. A `DashboardFreshnessEvaluator` existed but was used for one thing, not the cards.

`DashboardKpiFreshness.cs` (new, pure): `Describe(dataAtUtc, now, maxAge, scope)` returns the state (`fresh` / `stale` / `no-data`), a sentence, the relative time and the coverage. Two decisions worth naming: an **unknown** data time is not treated as current (it is its own state, worded differently from "we know it is old" -- those are different answers), and `AfterRefreshFailure` re-marks a card so the number on screen is labelled as predating a refresh that did not succeed. `ForSnapshot` builds all six cards, each from **its own** data time rather than the snapshot's render time. The scope line says what a card counts and refuses to be a place for source locations: anything containing `://`, a slash or a query is replaced with a generic descriptor rather than trimmed.

`DashboardData.cs`: `DashboardSnapshot` gained four additive, null-defaulted per-KPI timestamps, filled in `Load()` from the records themselves (`products.Max(UpdatedUtc)`, `orders.Max(UpdatedAt)`, `sync.Max(UpdatedUtc)`, the latest connection test) -- a snapshot from before this existed reports "veri zamanı bilinmiyor" instead of borrowing `GeneratedUtc`, which is when the board was drawn, not when the figure was last true. `DashboardPanel.cs`: cards render the state sentence and coverage beneath the number with a warning glyph and border when stale (words, not only colour), an automation name reading value + state + scope, and the catch block now re-renders the last snapshot's cards through `AfterRefreshFailure`.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DashboardKpiFreshnessTests.cs` (new, 4, red first): fresh, stale and missing timestamps each reading differently with the last two both counting as not-current; a failed refresh marking a one-minute-old card while keeping its scope; a scope carrying a signed feed URL replaced entirely (not even the host survives) while an ordinary description passes through; and a snapshot whose products are ten minutes old, orders three days old, sync unknown and XML four days old producing exactly fresh / stale / no-data / stale.

## #808 — dashboard anomaly cards (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-808-dashboard-anomaly-cards off codex/issue-807-kpi-freshness-indicators

New issue. ENTRY is the dashboard's anomaly projection. These situations were already surfaced -- as undifferentiated lines in the notification centre ("Kritik stok", "Sync başarısız · …"), each one sentence with no sense of cost, no age, and no way to tell which of twenty lines to act on first.

`DashboardAnomalies.cs` (new, pure): `Project(input, nowUtc)` turns the four states the app genuinely tracks -- oversell risk, a supplier feed gone quiet, failed dispatches, and order lines matching no product -- into cards carrying **impact** (what it costs in the operator's terms), **age** (how long it has been true) and **next action** with a route. No anomaly type is invented that the data cannot support. Counts and timestamps are supplied by the caller because those queries have their own owners; this decides severity, wording and order. Ordering is severity first -- overselling leads because it is the one that costs money and creates cancellations -- then oldest-first within a severity, since a month-old stale feed outranks a one-minute-old failure. A count of zero produces no card whatever timestamp is still lying beside it, so a resolved anomaly disappears rather than lingering at zero. Cards carry counts and a scope label only, and a scope that looks like a location is replaced outright.

`DashboardData.cs`: `DashboardSnapshot` gained the anomaly counts and their oldest occurrences (additive, defaulted), gathered while the snapshot is already reading those collections so the cards never re-query. "Oversell risk" is an *active* product with no stock -- a live listing that cannot be fulfilled -- and staleness reuses the same seven-day rule as the rest of the app. `DashboardPanel.cs`: a new "Anomaliler" group above the notification centre renders each card with its glyph, count, impact, age, scope and a button that navigates to where the fix happens, with a heavier border for the critical one and an automation name carrying title, count and age.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DashboardAnomalyCardsTests.cs` (new, 6, red first): a clean board saying so rather than looking broken; all four states becoming cards that each have an impact, an age, an action and a route; ordering by severity then age, checked with equal-age criticals and a month-old warning; a resolved anomaly with a leftover timestamp producing no card; the scope stripped when it looks like a signed URL and passed through when it is a shop label; and an anomaly with no known start refusing to claim an age.

Not done: the six-hour staleness window is one constant for every KPI, though an order count and a supplier feed age at very different rates -- per-KPI windows are a settings change; the "store switch" case from the issue's test list is covered by #783's per-directory cache (a different store is a different snapshot, and its own timestamps come with it) rather than by a test here; the quick-action cards (Kategoriler, Excel, Raporlar…) carry no freshness because they are navigation, not figures; no UI test of the rendered card (the panel is a projection of the tested model, and the failure path re-renders through the same function).

Not done: `AuditEvent` has no actor field, so "actor" in the issue's list is represented by the module (which subsystem acted) -- recording a real actor is an audit-store change and would only ever say "operator" or a job name in a single-user desktop app; there is no per-field diff in the timeline (the store keeps a free-text `Detail`, not before/after values -- #805 covers the pre-save diff, and a persisted field-level diff means writing one on every save); the whole audit table is read and filtered in memory per binding rather than queried by product (the store has no `ProductId` index; at the 5000-row retention limit this is cheap, but it is the obvious next step); no UI test of the expanders.

Not done: the diff is per field, not per word -- a one-character title fix shows both strings in full rather than highlighting the change, and an inline word diff is a rendering job (and a much bigger one) than this issue's "before-after preview"; the preview covers the four content fields the issue names, not price/stock (those have their own summaries in #798/#799); it is a dialog rather than an inline panel, which keeps it out of the card's way but means it cannot be watched while editing; no UI test of the dialog (the content is a projection of the tested model and the dialog has one button).

Not done: the tiles show a state glyph and host rather than an actual thumbnail image -- decoding 50 remote images into a strip needs the image cache #797 recorded as missing, and a strip that fetches every URL on selection would be worse than the table it replaces; the focus ring is the `ListBoxItem`'s default visual rather than a custom high-contrast-aware adorner; no UI test of the strip (the rules are covered, the wiring is a straight projection); the URL column remains in the grid (it is genuinely useful on screen) -- only the silent clipboard path was closed; `Shift+Arrow` range selection is not supported, the strip is single-select by design.

Not done: WPF binding errors (the red-outlined numeric fields, `ValidBindings`) are still a separate check that throws at save and are not folded into the panel -- reading them means walking the visual tree per field and mapping controls to sections, which is a card-structure change; the panel does not move focus to the offending *field*, only to its section (the field-level jump needs a control registry the card does not have); "stale result" is handled by re-evaluating on every refresh rather than by tracking evaluation age; the import path's own `Valid` call now produces per-field messages, which is an improvement but changes the text of some import errors.

Not done: the indicator updates on focus loss rather than per keystroke, because the card's bindings are `LostFocus` bindings -- making it live would mean switching them to `PropertyChanged`, which changes when validation fires and is a card-wide behaviour change; validation errors are not yet folded into the indicator (the issue lists them, but the card has no per-field validation surface to read -- `ValidBindings` throws at save time instead); the dot is appended to the tab header text rather than being a separate adorner, so a very narrow tab strip will elide it with the label; no breadcrumb, because the workspace has none yet.

Not done: `Navigate` does not yet parse the `products#section` fragment -- `ParseRoute` and `SelectProductSection` exist and are tested, but wiring them into the navigation router means touching the back/forward history stack, which deserves its own change; the section memory is per-session and per-workspace, not persisted or per-product; the `identity` section still contains the price and stock *editors* while `price-stock` shows them read-only -- splitting the editors out is a card restructure beyond this issue's navigation scope; no UI test of the focus move itself (the tab selection is covered, the `MoveFocus` call is not).

Not done: provenance is per *field group*, not per individual property -- title and description share the product-level `SourceKind` because the import does not stamp them separately, and stamping them would be an import change rather than a card one; the revision shown is the source's applied mapping revision at the time of reading, not a revision recorded on the product itself, so a product imported two mappings ago reports the source's current applied revision rather than its own (recording it per product is the honest fix and is an import-side change); no UI test of the expander (the render is a projection of the tested model); the four raw source columns remain in the grid alongside the new summary.

Not done: the margin here is `(price - cost) / price` -- deliberately the naive figure, labelled approximate, because computing the real contribution needs the channel's commission/shipping/VAT and that is the dispatch gate's job, not the card's; the "last calculation" time is `FxRateDate`, which is only stamped by the formula path, so a manually typed price reads as "Hesaplanmadı" (true, but it could distinguish "manually set" from "never calculated"); no UI test of the block (the render is a straight projection of the tested model); the thin-margin threshold is a constant rather than a per-channel setting.

Not done: the drawer reads the sync store on every open (a small query, but not cached) and does not refresh itself while open -- it is a snapshot taken when asked for, which is what "quick inspect" means here, but a long-lived open drawer will age; no image thumbnail in the drawer (that is #797's media fallback work, and pulling a remote image into a hover-level panel needs those states first); the drawer is a fixed 320 DIP rather than resizable; Ctrl+I is not yet advertised anywhere in the UI beyond the close button's label.

CI caught what the local run could not: the runner is en-US, where `N0` formats 1000 as "1,000", while this machine is tr-TR ("1.000") -- the test had the Turkish separator hard-coded and failed on the runner (294/295). The production formatting is right (a UI count belongs in the operator's locale); the expectation now computes the same string with the same format, so it holds in any culture. The same hard-coded-separator mistake was already in the #796 price test and was fixed with it.

Not done: selected/hover/focus are still WPF's own `DataGridRow` visuals -- the issue lists them with the data states, but overriding the row `ControlTemplate` to restyle selection would also override the focus adorner and the high-contrast selection colours the OS provides, which is a bigger and riskier change than this issue's value justifies; the accent brush is applied to the row border rather than a dedicated stripe element, so a theme that hides row borders would lose it; high contrast is read once per row realization (`SystemParameters.HighContrast`) and not re-read on a live theme switch; the seven-day staleness threshold is a constant, not a per-source setting.

## #809 — dashboard store filter persistence (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-809-dashboard-store-filter off codex/issue-808-dashboard-anomaly-cards

New issue. DESIGN: the board is read by an operator running several shops; until now it showed everything with no way to narrow it, and nothing about the view survived a restart.

`DashboardStoreFilter.cs` (new, pure) is two rules. `Options(candidates)` builds the offered list: "Tüm mağazalar" always first, then one entry per connection that exists **and** is enabled, keyed `channel|shopId` (channel lower-cased, duplicates and empty keys dropped) — a switched-off connection is not something the board can be pointed at, so it is not offered. `Resolve(savedKey, options)` is an **ordinal, exact lookup against that list**, not a parse of the saved string: a key that was never offered — however it got into the preference file — resolves to all-stores rather than being trusted, so a forged or stale value cannot aim the board at a store the operator cannot currently use. A saved store that has since been deleted or disabled falls back *visibly* (`FellBack` + a Turkish notice), because a board silently filtered to nothing is worse than one showing everything; never having chosen a store is not a fallback and says nothing. Each option also carries a human `Scope` label ("etsy / shop-a", "tüm mağazalar") with no storage key in it.

`DashboardPanel.cs`: a `ComboBox` docked right in the toolbar, backed by `UiPreferenceStore` under the data directory (so the choice is per store/profile and survives a restart). Every snapshot re-derives the options from `snapshot.Connections` and re-resolves the saved key; a fallback writes the notice to the status line **and** resets the stored preference so the dead key is not retried forever. An `applyingStoreFilter` guard keeps the programmatic `SelectedItem` assignment from re-entering the handler; a real selection persists the key and refreshes. The resolved scope now flows into `ShowAnomalies(..., scope)`, so #808's cards quote the filter instead of a hard-coded "tüm mağazalar". Preference writes are wrapped — a read-only profile directory must not break the dashboard.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DashboardStoreFilterTests.cs` (new, 6, red first): all-stores-first with disabled connections excluded; a saved store restored after a restart; deleted *and* disabled both falling back with a notice; nothing-saved (null/empty/whitespace) and a connection-less installation resolving quietly; forged keys (`etsy|shop-admin`, `*`, `all|*`, `etsy|shop-a extra`, `ETSY|SHOP-A`) never resolving to an unoffered store while the exact offered key still works; and the scope label being readable rather than the storage key.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=DashboardStoreFilter.cs (new), DashboardPanel.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DashboardStoreFilterTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=370 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 364); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 a37440ea5b94efe422170264b2ff88c915a08edf75ef50c8ee78ae0b26cecdf9.
Not done: the filter is a scope *label* handed to the cards, not a per-shop re-query — `DashboardDataService` reads a whole data directory and its counts are not shop-scoped, so choosing a store today narrows what the anomaly cards claim to cover, not the figures themselves; scoping the queries means giving every KPI a shop predicate and is the honest next issue. The KPI cards and the order trend still ignore the filter for the same reason. The selection is stored per data directory, so two profiles keep separate choices but two windows on the same profile share one. No "only stores with problems" quick filter and no multi-select (the issue asks for one scope at a time). The combo lists connections, not saved views — a saved view still owns filters, not the dashboard scope.

## #810 — dashboard drill-through breadcrumbs (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-810-drill-through-breadcrumbs off codex/issue-809-dashboard-store-filter

New issue. DESIGN: a KPI or anomaly card is a question with a context — the store the board was filtered to (#809) and the entity the card was counting (#808) — and following one threw both away. The window kept a `Stack<string>` of route keys, so Back returned to a *screen*, never to the view; and the breadcrumb printed `routeHistory.Reverse().Take(2)`, which shows the two **oldest** steps and never names the page you are on.

`NavigationDrillThrough.cs` (new, pure): `DrillTarget` (route, title, store key, entity kind/id/label), `DrillRequest` (a target plus the store keys this session offers) and `DrillThroughStack`. `Open` is the security line — a target naming a store outside the offered keys is refused (ordinal comparison against the offered list, never a parse of the target), and while the board is filtered to one store a target naming a *different* store is refused as a wrong-store deep link; an unscoped screen stays reachable either way, and a refusal leaves the trail untouched. `Back` returns the **whole** context of the crumb it lands on, filter included; landing on a crumb whose entity has since disappeared drops the entity, keeps its screen and says why, so a dead record is never re-opened; Back at the root stays at the root. `SwitchStore` clears a trail dug through the previous store's data (re-selecting the store you are on is not a switch). `TrailText` puts the current page last and elides the middle of a long trail with `…` while Back still walks every step.

`MainWindow.Navigation.cs`: `routeHistory` is gone — the stack is the trail. Sidebar navigation opens inside the board's current scope; `DrillThrough(request)` refuses a blocked link with a log line and no movement; `SwitchDashboardStore(key)` drops the trail and returns to the board; `Back_Click` goes through `Back(DrillEntityAlive)`, where liveness is a real lookup (`CatalogStore.FindProduct`, `OrdersStore.Find`) that fails open on error rather than trapping the operator. `DashboardPanel.cs`: KPI and anomaly cards now drill with their entity and the board's scope, and the store combo notifies the window before reloading. `MainWindow.UX.cs`: Alt+Left walks the trail from the keyboard.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NavigationDrillThroughTests.cs` (new, 6, red first): nested drill-down with the current page last; Back restoring route *and* filter *and* entity, then the root, then refusing to underflow; a vanished entity degrading to its screen with a notice; a store switch clearing the trail while a same-store re-selection keeps it; forged/cross-store keys never opening; and a long trail eliding its middle. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DashboardDrillThroughWindowTests.cs` (new, 3): the same three behaviours through a real `MainWindow` — the breadcrumb text an operator reads, the back button's enabled state, and a wrong-store deep link leaving the window on the dashboard.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=NavigationDrillThrough.cs (new), MainWindow.Navigation.cs, MainWindow.UX.cs, DashboardPanel.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NavigationDrillThroughTests.cs (new), tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DashboardDrillThroughWindowTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=379 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 370); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 e90e7970fbe8dbe5baf530afacbd19f6284266343d71fa726e09dc00f58ac704.
Not done: the crumb carries the entity but the landing screen does not yet *select* it — drilling into "Aşırı satış riski" opens the product list, it does not pre-filter it to the risky rows, because each screen owns its own filter state and routing a context into it is a per-screen change (the target already carries everything those screens need). The trail is not clickable: crumbs render as text and only the last step is reachable, via Back or Alt+Left. It is in-memory, so it does not survive a restart the way `last-route` does. Liveness is checked only for products and orders — every other entity kind is assumed alive rather than pretending to a check that does not exist. `DrillEntityAlive` opens a store per Back press (cheap, single-row lookups, no cache).

## #811 — dashboard zero-data onboarding state (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-811-dashboard-zero-data-onboarding off codex/issue-810-drill-through-breadcrumbs

New issue. DESIGN: an empty board showed a grid of zeros and the status line's "Yerel veriler yüklendi", which is the same answer for five different situations and useful in none of them.

`DashboardEmptyState.cs` (new, pure): `Evaluate(input, routeExists)` is a ladder that answers the *first* thing missing: no marketplace connection (`NO_STORE` → connections), a store but no XML source (`NO_SOURCE` → xml), a source that has never run (`NEVER_RUN` → xml, "önizle"), a source that has run but never delivered a successful feed (`DISCONNECTED_SOURCE` → xml, "yeniden bağla" — a different problem from unstarted, and the issue's "disconnected source" case), a filter hiding data that exists (`FILTERED_EMPTY` → dashboard, naming the scope), and a complete setup that simply produced no rows (`NO_RECORDS` → products). A board with data returns `NONE` and renders nothing. The "unsupported action" rule is the caller-supplied `routeExists`: a step whose screen this build does not have keeps its diagnosis and **drops the button** rather than promising a capability; nothing in the copy names an endpoint, a token or a marketplace operation.

`DashboardData.cs`: two additive snapshot fields, `SourcesEverRun` (`LastRunUtc` set) and `SourcesWithSuccessfulFeed` (`LastSuccessfulFeedUtc` set), read from the sources the snapshot already loads. `DashboardPanel.cs`: an onboarding host above the KPI cards, filled by `ShowEmptyState` on every refresh from the snapshot, the store filter (#809: filtered = a specific store; visible = that store's connection rows) and the resolved scope label; collapsed when the board has data; the action button navigates through the ordinary route. `MainWindow.Navigation.cs` passes `key => routes.ContainsKey(key)` so the "screen exists" check is the window's real route table, not a copy of it.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DashboardEmptyStateTests.cs` (new, 7, red first): the four issue reasons each with a distinct sentence, a safe action and a known route; a working board saying nothing; ran-but-empty diagnosed as disconnected rather than unstarted; a missing screen dropping the button while keeping the diagnosis; no forbidden vocabulary (`http`, `api.`, `/v1`, `token`, `endpoint`) in any state's copy and every action landing on a known route; the ladder preferring "connect a store" over "your filter is too narrow" when nothing is set up; and — through a real `CatalogStore` + `MarketplaceConnectionStore` + `DashboardDataService.Load` — a source flipping from `NEVER_RUN` to `DISCONNECTED_SOURCE` once it has a `LastRunUtc` and no successful feed.

REAL_WORK_COUNT=1; FILES_CHANGED=DashboardEmptyState.cs (new), DashboardData.cs, DashboardPanel.cs, MainWindow.Navigation.cs, tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/DashboardEmptyStateTests.cs (new), IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md; TEST_RESULT=386 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (full Release suite, up from 379); Release build and self-contained win-x64 publish succeeded; artifact SHA-256 2ab006b7a48a760344d63e6c138af86752d68b6b6d534eaae3ae09ee87b99c51.
Not done: `FILTERED_EMPTY` on the dashboard is driven by the only collection the store filter genuinely scopes today (connection rows), because the KPI queries are not shop-scoped (#809's open item) — it fires when the selected store has no connection row left, not when a store has no products; that becomes real once the snapshot is per-shop. "Disconnected" is inferred from "ran, never a successful feed", not from a live reachability probe, so a source whose credentials broke *after* a good run reads as stale (#808) rather than disconnected — the two are distinct states and the copy does not conflate them. The onboarding wizard (`OnboardingPanel.ShowWizard`) is not launched from here; the empty state routes to the screens, it does not start the wizard. No UI test of the rendered panel (the projection is tested; the panel is a straight rendering of it).

## #812 — navigation sidebar collapse persistence (2026-09-13), branch codex/issue-812-sidebar-collapse-persistence off codex/issue-811-dashboard-zero-data-onboarding

New issue. DESIGN: the shell's sidebar was a fixed 214-DIP column with no way to narrow it, and no state of it survived a restart.

`NavigationSidebarState.cs` (new, pure): `NavigationSidebarState(Collapsed, ExpandedWidth)` with `NavigationSidebar.Parse`/`Serialize` (JSON under `UiPreferenceStore` key `shell:sidebar`), `Toggle`, `Resize` (clamped to 160–360 DIP; a drag while collapsed changes nothing, since nothing was dragged open), `CurrentWidth` (56 DIP when collapsed) and `Present(title, description, collapsed)`. The stored value is untrusted: garbage, a non-object, a non-numeric or out-of-band width all fall back to the **default** rather than being clamped into a width nobody chose. Widths are DIPs — the unit WPF lays out in — so a DPI change re-renders the same split instead of drifting it; there is no pixel conversion to drift through. In icons-only mode `Present` prints a one- or two-letter glyph built with **Turkish casing** ("Ürün yönetimi" → "ÜY", "İlk kurulum" → "İK", a one-word title gets one letter), while `ToolTip` and `AccessibleName` are the full title in both modes — the label leaves the screen, not the accessibility tree.

`MainWindow.xaml`: the sidebar column is named, a toggle button sits above the brand, and a `GridSplitter` on the column's right edge allows the resize the issue asks for. `MainWindow.Navigation.cs`: `ApplySidebarState()` is the single renderer (column width/min/max, splitter enabled, brand/footer/search visibility, group headers hidden when collapsed, every entry's content, tooltip and `AutomationProperties.Name` through `Present`), used by toggle, drag-complete and startup alike; `SaveSidebarState()` persists and logs rather than throws on a read-only profile; menu search now matches on the entry's label rather than on `Content`, which in icons-only mode is the glyph. `MainWindow.UX.cs`: Ctrl+B toggles.

`tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NavigationSidebarStateTests.cs` (new, 5, red first): restart through a real `UiPreferenceStore` restoring collapsed *and* the open width; eight garbage/hostile saved values all yielding a usable default; resize clamping and the collapsed no-op; a fractional DIP width round-tripping exactly with no pixel unit in the payload; and icons-only presentation keeping name and tooltip with Turkish-cased glyphs. `tests/MarketplaceHub.Tests/NavigationSidebarWindowTests.cs` (new, 1): the real shell — collapsing to 56 DIP, the search box hidden, every entry keeping its accessible name and tooltip and printing a glyph, group headers hidden, an icons-only entry taking real keyboard focus, and a second `MainWindow` over the same directory opening collapsed and expanding back to the width in use.
